## 53. BND-09: exact chronology and typing boundary

1. **[CONST]** `P` is already constituted and *occurring* for the compatible-family corollary (4010), but the occurrence of `P` does not actualize each solution member or an independent state/history (2172–2187; 2232–2257; 3964–3970). Carrier versus member is a genuine sort distinction, not a missing physical axiom.
2. **[FROZEN] [GEN]** A constructor word `w` is a physical origin certificate only if every intermediate constructor, input, output sort, retention and reuse is licensed *before* the evaluated result, as in `p2a:def:frozen-constructor-origin-certificate` 2197–2230. Pure mathematical availability and frozen-looking notation do not by themselves add a physical constructor. The Batch 9 targets explicitly premise a licensed/frozen constructor and generated quotient/readout; applications must supply the actual word.
3. **[GEN]** The family consists of **complete** GP1-admissible, GP2-zero-defect downstream realizations; any quotient congruence is result-independent, generated, predeclared and preserves host/role typing. The stronger source theorem 5101–5129 additionally requires the quotient itself in its stated GP1 finite/exact domain; the orbit quotient corollary 4117–4121 explicitly guards infinite totally-bounded quotients. These restrictions are antecedents to an *applicable* family theorem, not new GP4 axioms.
4. **[MIN]** GP3 compares the **complete candidate preorder** (3715–3768). The original candidate quotient and the GP3-retained output are different typed objects. A unique least class may exist even if multiple initial compatible classes exist (4134). This distinction is necessary for the negative direction of a readout criterion.
5. **[DER] [REAL]** A licensed readout `O` on the retained output has a determined common value only if its image is a singleton **and the retained family is nonempty** (5133–5146). It determines no newly occurring token and does not select an element of an unresolved source family. Empty exact families are FRC-obstructed, not a way of deriving an arbitrary value.
6. **[DER]** Tangent annihilation in 4090–4098 is only for actual velocities of admissible curves in a smooth stratum. Kernel of the linearized constraints is a valid substitute *only if* integrability/regularity is certified. Connected components may carry different constants.
7. **[ID] [CAL]** No numerical calibration, preferred representative, inferred probability measure, or physical history is promoted by quotienting. A dimension/count readout in a test model is a mathematical control, not a framework-predicted observed number.

## 54. Independent replay of the three valid deductions

**Carrier/member:** `p2a:thm:generated-least-carrier` applies the role-faithful complete-candidate preorder to a generated carrier `Car_X(S)`. The theorem contains no constructor of states, histories or actions in its output sort. From `Car_X(S)` alone, the implication to a *particular* `m ∈ Car_X(S)` would require a selection/occurrence word not provided. This is precisely the GP2 declared-output firewall, so lines 3973–3979 follow without extra assumptions.

**Chronology:** If `Orig_Σ(w;D,Q)` holds, all of its licensed source steps occur before output evaluation, and `ReqOut_Σ(w,D)` is in the generated closure. By the rooted generated-image co-realization theorem, its **role-level** output is retained on the nonobstructed branch. GP3 compares faithful complete realizations afterwards, never arbitrary numerical simplicity of output values. Thus later calculated consequences of an action cannot be witnesses that retroactively license the same action constructor. This proves lines 3982–4006 within the stated antecedent, not for every imaginable action.

**Common-value theorem (valid positive direction):** Let `Q=F(P)/~_P` be a generated, nonempty, GP1-admissible complete quotient with licensed origin. Let `S_GP3(Q)` denote the resulting nonempty retained least class or unresolved irreducible classes, as appropriate under the source GP3 rule. For a generated, typed readout `O:S_GP3(Q)->Y`, if `O(S_GP3(Q))={y*}`, the readout graph has singleton image and `y*` is generated common content by `p2a:thm:generated-exact-solution-moduli` (iii). The source does not require `S_GP3(Q)` to have exactly one realization. This positive result is also true under the *stronger sufficient* premise that `O` is constant on **all** initial compatible classes, provided the family is nonempty and admitted. A nonconstant readout on initial classes, conversely, is **not** evidence of nonconstancy on the retained output.

**Local tangent rule:** On an admitted piecewise `C^1` path `q(s)` in the smooth stratum, `q'(s)` is an actual tangent. If `DO_q(q'(s))=0` everywhere, the chain rule yields `d/ds O(q(s))=0`; thus `O` is constant on the stratum's path-connected components. At a point with `DO_q(v)≠0` for an *actual* curve tangent `v`, an admitted curve witnesses local variation. A nonintegrable formal linearized kernel vector alone is insufficient. A componentwise result does not collapse disconnected components into one class.

## 55. New gap certificates — precisely two local scope defects

### BH-IIA-QUOT-EMPTY-001 — missing nonempty-family guard

- **Target:** `p2a:thm:compatible-family-observable-closure`, v3.206 **4010–4039**, especially **4022–4027**.
- **Consumed upstream:** `p2a:thm:generated-exact-solution-moduli` (i),(iii), **5133–5146**, proof **5170–5188**, which distinguishes nonempty from FRC-obstructed exact loci and assumes `nonempty M` for the singleton-readout inference. GP3 explicit empty-class branch **3768–3770**.
- **Missing edge:** `∃ y* ∀f∈Q, O(f)=y* ⇒ y* generated` requires `Q≠∅` (and valid typed generated readout) for this result. The corollary does **not** list that hypothesis in its general readout clause.
- **Exact failure:** For an empty exact solution locus `Q=∅`, the unique function `O:∅->{0,1}` satisfies the stated equality for **both** `y*=0` and `y*=1`. No unique observable follows; the role is instead FRC-obstructed. A frozen inconsistent finite exact constraint diagram (e.g. `x=0` and `x=1`) is a control illustrating the allowed empty branch; it does not claim an actual contradictory physical occurrence. A syntactically generated empty readout is not a physical value.
- **Why required:** The convenience proof **4034–4038** invokes the *nonempty* clause of the stronger theorem without importing its guard. The failure is a quantifier/typing proof error, **not a refutation** of the upstream theorem or the valid positive direction on nonempty families.
- **Repair:** Add `Assume Q is nonempty` to common-value conclusion; if `Q=∅`, conclude FRC obstruction and abstain from assigning any `O*`.
- **Affected:** the local generic corollary on empty families; no reviewed direct external consumer relied on an empty-fibre singleton inference. **POSSIBLY_AFFECTED** any future consumer failing to prove that its solution fibre is inhabited; direct citations are not proof-use certification.
- **Status:** **SOURCE-LEVEL SCOPE ERROR CONFIRMED** (E1+E3+exact finite E4), medium local severity, no physical counterexample asserted.

### BH-IIA-QUOT-SURV-001 — initial-candidate versus GP3-survivor quantifier

- **Target:** `p2a:thm:compatible-family-observable-closure`, v3.206 **4028–4030** (`if two compatible realizations give different readout values, the parent does not determine that observable`).
- **Consumed upstream:** GP3 **3755–3768**; `p2:cor:generated-orbit-family-inheritance` explicitly **4128–4135** states that multiple pre-GP3 quotient classes do *not* preclude a unique least class; `p2a:thm:generated-exact-solution-moduli` **5138–5146** uses the **surviving** class domain for readout determination.
- **Missing edge:** `∃q1,q2∈Q (O(q1)≠O(q2)) ⇒ no determined GP3 output` is false without `q1,q2∈S_GP3(Q)`. The quantifier silently crosses from all initial complete candidates to the GP3 survivor output.
- **Exact finite control:** For the one-unit algebra role, take finite exact `A0=C` and `A1=C⊕C`, with the extra factor unused by the constituted role. The unital *-homomorphism `(λ,μ)→λ` is a role-faithful reduction of the kind allowed by GP3's **unused-factor** comparison clause (3742–3753), provided the declared role contains no mark requiring that factor; the candidate-internal extra test is not promoted to the frozen inventory. With frozen readout `O(A)=dim_C A`, the two initial readouts are `1,2`. GP3's unique least candidate is `A0` with determined value `1`. The readout is a predeclared, licensed **mathematical control** for the quantifier test, not an experimentally asserted observable or a new GP3 dimension selector. The exact comparison/homomorphism calculation is in the Batch 9 check script.
- **Why required:** The source's stronger theorem specifically refers to every *surviving* class, and its proof of the nonselection conclusion uses that domain (5141–5146, 5182–5188). The convenience corollary incorrectly extends its negative conclusion to all pre-GP3 compatible realizations.
- **Repair:** In the negative conclusion, replace `two compatible realizations` by `two distinct GP3-surviving classes of the complete currently retained output` **with a licensed readout defined on both**. If these differ, no singleton common readout is determined by the current data; GP3 must not choose a member *in order to force* a desired value. Earlier nonleast candidates cannot be used for that obstruction.
- **Affected:** local convenience corollary negative direction. Source direct consumers verified only on their positive singleton/constant-image branches: Paper III-A **3919** and **5118**; Paper V-C **3170**; Paper V-D **7380**. They are not shown defective by this finding. Paper V-D **7353** uses the separate local tangent rule, which remains valid. Other unreviewed consumers remain `POSSIBLY_AFFECTED` until their actual role and survivor sets are inspected.
- **Status:** **SOURCE-LEVEL SCOPE ERROR CONFIRMED** (E1+E3+exact finite E4), medium local severity; no physical alternate root occurrence asserted.

The preceding defects are **independent**: an inhabited family can still have discarded nonleast candidates, while the empty-family problem exists even if the GP3 preorder is trivial. They deserve two canonical scoped issue IDs, not duplicate copies of the earlier full-parent Lie-port issue.

## 56. Corrective lemma (derived using existing framework, not an additional axiom)

**Claim.** Suppose a predeclared, generated family constructor with a frozen `Orig_Σ` word produces a **complete**, GP1-admissible, GP2-exact quotient `Q` for an already occurring owner `P`, and its congruence and generated readout sorts are licensed. Then:

1. If `Q=∅`, the role is FRC-obstructed: neither GP3 nor a vacuous constant-map identity produces a readout value.
2. If `Q≠∅`, GP3 retains a unique least structured class where one exists, or otherwise its unresolved surviving family `S_GP3(Q)` in the exact source sense. Carrier-level co-realization does not actualize a particular member.
3. If a licensed readout `O` is defined on `S_GP3(Q)` and `O(S_GP3(Q))={y*}`, then `y*` is generated common content with no member selection.
4. If `O(S_GP3(Q))` contains at least two distinct values, there is no unique common value determined **by the current retained family and readout alone**. No GP3 selection *merely for that numeric output* is licensed. A later independently generated constraint may change the candidate family and must be audited separately.

**Proof.** (1) is the empty exact-moduli branch of `p2a:thm:generated-exact-solution-moduli` (i). (2) is GP3's whole-candidate rule and the moduli theorem (ii). (3) is moduli theorem (iii), after the typed quotient/family origin certificate and nonempty hypothesis. (4) is the contrapositive of singleton-image determination *relative to the current family*, without claiming GP3 is unable to choose a least class for independent structural reasons. The constructors and time ordering are exactly those in the original source. □

The repair uses no new GP, no full-carrier uniqueness demand, no arbitrary member selection and no reproof of `H_1` or `m_p=1`. It belongs as a **proof/scope correction** to Paper II-A when the framework is next edited. **The framework .tex/.pdf is not changed by this audit.**

## 60. Independently checked proofs

### 60.1 Uniformly continuous quotient comparison

Let `(X,d)` be totally bounded, `q:X→Y` be a uniformly continuous surjection onto a generated quotient comparison with metric/uniform structure `ρ`. Given `ε>0`, choose `δ>0` from uniform continuity. A finite `δ`-net `{x_i}` of `X` exists. For any `y=q(x)` one has some `i` with `d(x,x_i)<δ`, whence `ρ(y,q(x_i))<ε`. The finitely many `q(x_i)` form an `ε`-net of `Y`. This is exactly the extra comparison-control hypothesis in the source's 4117–4121. **Result: GP1 total boundedness passes to the specified quotient**; this proof says nothing about arbitrary set quotients with different uniformities.

**Adversarial boundary:** `X={1/n : n≥1} ⊂ R` is totally bounded, but the map `f(1/n)=n` has unbounded image. It is not uniformly continuous: `|1/n−1/(n+1)|→0`, while `|f(1/n)−f(1/(n+1))|=1`. Therefore GP1 cannot be inferred without the required continuity/comparison certificate. This is a mathematical guard test, not a countermodel to the actual, guarded orbit corollary.

### 60.2 Exact-solution moduli (i) and GP3 branch (ii)

The frozen `Orig_Σ` predicate at 2197–2230 requires every constructor used (including formation of *complete* solution family and the quotient) to be licensed at the frozen stage, with input roots and target sort declared before evaluation and no candidate-specific selection. When the resulting quotient is GP1-admissible and the source locus is nonempty, the orbit-family corollary makes the family a generated role descendant. Rooted co-realization 2232–2278 then retains that **role-level** output, *if* the jointly occurring/generated input tuple discharges its `RootReal_P(D)` condition. The source antecedent describes `D` precisely as jointly occurring/generated; it must be read as including this root/co-realized input condition and not as independently requiring a new G0 witness for each solution. If the full exact locus is empty, joint-family exhaustion 4972–5041 and GP3 3768–3770 declare FRC obstruction, not an arbitrary generated value. On the nonempty branch, GP3 3755–3768 retains unique least *if one exists*; otherwise its source-rule is unresolved irreducible classes, not a chosen member. No global existence of an exact-solution family is inferred from the abstract theorem alone.

### 60.3 Readout (iii): correct positive conclusion and bounded proof overreach

Take nonempty admitted exact moduli `M`, and let `S_GP3(M)` denote the nonempty actually retained least class or unresolved irreducible family. If a predeclared licensed readout `R:M→Y` is congruence-respecting and `R(s)=y*` for all `s∈S_GP3(M)`, the readout on the retained family has image `{y*}` and **determines a common descendant value without a selected representative**. This is correct with no unique source member (E3 control: two incomparable survivors carrying the same readout value). This result does not use GP3 comparison-isomorphism of a *newly enlarged readout graph*.

However proof lines **5185–5188** additionally say that projection of the generated readout graph to the source family "is still an isomorphism and cannot select a member." If `isomorphism` means an isomorphism of **complete GP1/GP2-admissible GP3 candidate preorders**, the source itself states the opposite general boundary at **5005–5011 and 5051–5064**: graph bijection is only set-theoretic; comparison-preservation/reflection and conservative admissibility require separate checks. The source even provides an explicit conditional example at **5077–5093**: two initial candidates `a,b` have readout families `f(a)={0}`, `f(b)=N`; both graph pairs exist as a set, but when the complete generated output is consumed as physical family, GP1 excludes `b`. In such a case the graph is not an admissible-order isomorphism and does change surviving candidates, despite set-level bijection. This example is **not asserted** to satisfy all premises of the common-singleton-readout branch; it directly tests the *unqualified proof step*, not its correct common-value conclusion.

**Adjudication:** `BH-IIA-MOD-GRAPH-001` is a **confirmed local proof-scope overstatement**, not a proved failure of the main common-readout theorem. A chronology/conservativity qualification is needed *only if* the author intends to claim the readout graph preserves the GP3 preorder or never changes the admissible candidate domain. If "isomorphism" merely denotes the graph-to-domain bijection **of sets**, its first half is valid but cannot alone justify the stated no-selection step. The source already contains the exact required branch at 5051–5064; no additional GP is proposed.

### 60.4 Relation-free products, equalizers and the parent lift (iv)

Under factorized joint GP1/GP2 admissibility, with neither generated cross relation nor newly enabled common-constructor obligation, product admissibility holds by the actual GP2 relation-free composition clause. Where a generated equality `r1(q1)=r2(q2)` is consumed, the **exact** joint solution set is the intersection of that equality locus with every prior incidence and GP1/typing/constructor obligation, as in 5153–5159. It is the *ordinary* fibre product only if the other constraints are redundant there. A mere common comparison codomain does not imply equality. If the joint family is defined as a common-parent image, the standard fibre product is exhausted **if and only if** every compatible tuple has a common lift. This is the exact `p2a:lem:repair-joint-lift-completeness` statement 5652–5670.

**Exact control:** with `P={(0,0),(1,1)}`, projections `a_i:P→{0,1}` separately surjective, and both comparison maps constant into a one-point `C`, the fibre product has four pairs whereas the image of `(a1,a2)` has two. Thus separate surjectivity is insufficient. With additional retained prior incidence requiring `(0,0)`, even a two-pair equality fibre product is reduced to one allowed tuple. Both warnings are *already in the source* and are verified, not new framework gaps.

**Direct imports and provenance (not proof substitutes):**

Nothing in the target theorem asserts these physical data exist for an arbitrary owner. The first certificate must be supplied by the physical constructor of the intended consumer. This is an **explicit antecedent**, not a gap in the conditional theorem.
2. `[MATHEMATICAL IMPLICATION]` Positive-definite `G_0` gives a fixed Riesz identification; the symmetric Hessian gives a self-adjoint finite operator `C`, with orthogonal `V=N⊕E`, `N=ker C`. Its complementary restriction `C_E=C|E` is invertible. Source proof 5250–5259 then uses the standard finite-dimensional implicit-function theorem; no new physical field is inserted.
3. `[MATHEMATICAL IMPLICATION]` The complement graph solves `P_E ∇^0_z S=0`. On that graph all `E`-directional derivatives of `S` vanish. The envelope identity in 5261–5274 gives `d_n Φ=d_zS|N`; hence the exact full critical relation and inverse of solution sets. This proves the theorem **without** assuming a regular solution manifold or a unique branch `z=z_*(w)`.
4. `[C2 ANALYTIC CERTIFICATE]` Source 5269–5272 uses `S∈C²` and `ψ∈C¹` to infer `dΦ∈C¹` from the envelope identities. Therefore `Φ∈C²`. This is valid; a naive rule `C²∘C¹=C¹` would be inapplicable because **stationarity cancels the lower-regularity derivative of ψ**.
5. `[GENERATED THEOREM REUSE]` The local relation may be reused only under `p2a:def:v368-theorem-closure-status` 4860–4883 and `p2a:cor:v176-licensed-theorem-generated-frontier` 4885–4910, once the extra analytic `C²` certification and target sort are discharged. Source corollary 5279–5314 explicitly retains original owner constraints; it does **not** make `N` an autonomous physical subcarrier.
6. `[C3 / C4 ANALYTIC CERTIFICATES]` The constraint-jet proposition assumes a `C³` constraint and a `C³` actual solution curve. The quartic proposition assumes the action is `C⁴` and explicitly holds `w` fixed. Neither regularity nor solution-curve existence follows from G0 or GP1–GP3 merely through correct provenance. Both are source-declared conditions, not new principles.

**No dependency on `BH-IIA-PORT-001`:** the proof uses finite coordinate calculus on an already generated action, not universal Lie regularity of all full geometric parents. None of the three other historical quotient/moduli issues is a consumed premise in this finite critical proof.

## 66. E3 independent proof replay: exact critical relation

Let `F(w,n,e)=P_E∇^0_z S(w,z_0+n+e)` in the **fixed** inherited `G_0` metric. Then `F` is `C¹` because `S∈C²`; `F(w0,0,0)=0`; and its `e` derivative is `C_E`. Because `C` is self-adjoint, `C(E)⊂E` and `ker C_E=E∩ker C={0}`. Finite dimension implies `C_E:E→E` is an isomorphism. The implicit-function theorem yields a unique `C¹` local function `ψ(w,n)` solving `F=0`. Its graph has the same solutions as the complement equation; it is **not** an independently chosen dynamical graph.

Define `Φ(w,n)=S(w,z_0+n+ψ(w,n))`. For `η∈N` and `ξ` in the retained `w` directions,

\[
D_n\Phi[\eta]=D_zS[\eta+D_n\psi\,\eta]=D_zS[\eta],\qquad
D_w\Phi[\xi]=D_wS[\xi]+D_zS[D_w\psi\,\xi]=D_wS[\xi],
\]

since `Dψ` lies in `E` and `D_zS|_E=0`. These derivatives are `C¹` (composition of `C¹` functions `dS` with `C¹` graph), hence `Φ` is `C²`. Because `V=N⊕E`, `d_zS=0` iff its `E` component vanishes and its `N` component vanishes. The former yields `e=ψ`; the latter is exactly `d_n Φ=0`. The forward and inverse maps are explicitly

\[
(w,n)\mapsto(w,z_0+n+\psi(w,n)),\qquad
(w,z)\mapsto(w,P_N(z-z_0)).
\]

They are mutually inverse on the local solution loci, including singular sets. **The theorem's full local stationary set is preserved, not approximated.** Degenerate limits `E=0` and `N=0` are consistent with the zero-dimensional implicit-function theorem: the first keeps the entire critical equation; the second solves all interior variables by IFT.

## 67. E3 independent proof replay: jets and effective quartic

For `C(γ(s))=0`, let `J=DC_q`, `v=γ'(0)`, `a=γ''(0)`, `b=γ'''(0)`. Repeated differentiation yields

\[
Jv=0,\quad Ja+D^2C[v,v]=0,\quad Jb+3D^2C[v,a]+D^3C[v,v,v]=0.
\]

Projecting the second identity to `coker J` gives the necessary vanishing of `[D²C[v,v]]`. No converse holds: the source's smooth flat `C(x)=e^{-1/x²}` for `x≠0`, `C(0)=0` has **all jets zero at 0** but zero locus `{0}`; source 5328–5332 correctly rules out a false integrability inference.

For the quartic result, fix `w=w0` and expand `S(n,e)` at the stationary point. With `T=D³S`, `Q=D⁴S`, `C_E=C|E` and `⟨t(n,n),e⟩=T[n,n,e]`, the `E` stationary equation has leading terms `C_Ee + 1/2 t(n,n) + O(|n|³+|n||e|+|e|²)`, hence

\[
\psi(n)=-\tfrac12 C_E^{-1}t(n,n)+O(|n|^3).
\]

Substituting `e_2=-1/2 C_E^{-1}t(n,n)` into the action, the order-four complement contribution is

\[
\tfrac12\langle C_Ee_2,e_2\rangle +\tfrac12 T[n,n,e_2]
=-\tfrac18\langle t(n,n),C_E^{-1}t(n,n)\rangle.
\]

Thus

\[
\Phi(n)=S(0)+\tfrac16 T[n^3]+\tfrac1{24}\left(Q[n^4]-3\langle t(n,n),C_E^{-1}t(n,n)\rangle\right)+o(|n|^4).
\]

The source's `C⁴` assumption ensures enough regularity of `ψ` and the envelope derivative to justify the expansion. If `T|_{N³}=0` and `Q_eff[n⁴]≥c|n|⁴`, the radial derivative `dΦ(n)[n]=Q_eff[n⁴]/6+o(|n|⁴)` is positive for all sufficiently small nonzero `n`, proving **isolation**. Positivity of the transverse `C_E` additionally proves a **strict local minimum**, but a nondegenerate *quadratic* kernel mass is not inferred. If `C_E` is indefinite, positivity of `Q_eff` does **not** imply a minimum; the theorem and proposition correctly separate these claims.

## 68. E4 exact and adversarial controls

Executed `Proofs/Paper2A-FiniteCriticalBatch11-v1_1-verify.py`, producing `Proofs/Paper2A-FiniteCriticalBatch11-v1_1-results.json`, **15/15 checks PASS** after correcting two *test-script symbolic equality* mismatches during construction; no source theorem was changed. These are exact symbolic/finite controls, not physical-observation validation.

**Branching exact model:**
\[
S(w,n,e)=e^2+en^2+\tfrac12n^4-\tfrac12wn^2,
\quad\psi=-\tfrac12 n^2,
\quad\Phi=\tfrac14n^4-\tfrac12wn^2.
\]
The stationary condition `n(n²−w)=0` has `n=0, ±√w` for `w>0` and merges at `w=0`. **Every branch** lifts through `e=−n²/2`; no unique physical history is selected.

**Nonlinear cancellation / isolation:** at fixed `w=0`, the model `S=(a/2)e²+gen²+(λ/4)n⁴` reduces to `Φ=(λ/4−g²/(2a))n⁴`. The exact effective quartic is `Q_eff=6λ−12g²/a`, matching the paper. For `a=2,g=1,λ=1`, `Φ=0` and the critical curve survives. For `a=−2,g=1,λ=1`, `Φ=n⁴/2` has an isolated critical point but negative transverse direction `S(0,e)=−e²`: **not a local minimum**.

**Constraint jet example:** `C(x,y)=y−x²−x³` and `γ(s)=(s,s²+s³)` satisfy every displayed first/second/third jet relation exactly. An independent owner incidence such as `n=w` survives complement elimination: it becomes an added constraint on the reduced critical locus, not a choice of a preferred branch.

The controls also exercise the nondegenerate complement/kernel-zero boundary and source-file pin. **No in-domain counterexample to the four inspected claims was found.** Absence of a found counterexample is not an exhaustive universal guarantee; E3 provides the actual proof.

## 72. E3 independent mathematical replay of the same target

Let `Gen_Sigma(D_*)` be the minimal result-independent closure under finite typed constructor words already in the frozen grammar. Choose a topological ordering of the source's **finite, chronologically licensed dependency diagram**. For a sibling node, the frozen certificate `w_a` and its required native-sort output imply `P_a∈Gen_Sigma(D_*)`. Suppose all input nodes of a recursive step lie in that closure. Under (a), the stage output was already correctly retained in its declared sort; under (b), `R` is defined on a nonempty complete surviving family and has constant image `{q_n}` (equivalently factors through the singleton readout). In either case, the stage datum `Q_n` is a previously retained typed output; the independently certified word `v_n` therefore gives

`P_(n+1)=ReqOut_Sigma(v_n;(P_n,Q_n))∈Gen_Sigma(D_*)`.

If the step's GP1/GP2 exact candidate set is nonempty, GP2 closure forces the output in every candidate; the imported co-realization theorem then gives the *same rooted* occurrence. If empty, the theorem explicitly stops and makes no unconditional existence claim. A separately asserted family-valued node has its own frozen certified word `w_Delta`, so composing the finite words again places the **whole family object** (not an arbitrarily chosen fibre) in the rooted closure. This proves the precisely stated conditional cascade independently of the paper's sentence-by-sentence induction.

For a later family port, regard the nonempty exact moduli `M` as a single typed object in `FAMILY`, and let the licensed `v_fam:(P_n,M)→P_(n+1)^fam` preserve fibres. Finite word composition proves closure; no map `M→{m_*}` is supplied. Thus no section or probability measure can be deduced. An unresolved member-dependent readout that takes two values has image cardinality ≥2 and cannot fill a **scalar unique/common output** port. It can feed a later **family-valued port** only if that distinct word is licensed.

For joint owner data `X_i`, let `A_joint⊆∏ X_i` be the complete generated admissibility predicate and `R_alpha` all retained relations. GP2 exactness requires

`J=A_joint∩⋂_alpha R_alpha`.

Adding a separately generated common-value equation replaces this by `J=A_joint∩R_prior∩Eq(r_i)`; it equals the raw fibre product `Eq(r_i)` **if and only if** its other predicates are redundant there. This is the corollary's exact conclusion; no equality is inferred from maps having the same codomain.

For termination, if each nonstationary recursive step is a strict enlargement in one fixed finite poset/lattice `L`, there are at most `height(L)-1` such steps. A **finite-dimensional carrier** does not imply this antecedent: the one-dimensional subspaces `span_C((1,t))⊆C²`, with distinct real `t`, are infinitely many distinct subspaces because `det((1,t),(1,s))=s−t`. This is an E4 control on an invalid *stronger inference*, not a physical counterexample to the source theorem.

## 78. E3 independent replay of the mathematical implications

**(1) Existential relation image.** On the retained product domain `X`, let `π:X→D` and `R⊆X`. A tuple `d` is in `π(R)` iff `∃x∈R: π(x)=d`. The pullback `π⁻¹π(R)` is the union of the π-fibres meeting R. Therefore `R=π⁻¹π(R)` iff every such fibre lies entirely in R (saturation). This does **not** assert that the whole abstract compatibility fibre product is generated; `p2a:lem:repair-joint-lift-completeness` separately characterizes when it is exhausted.

**(2) Functionality and structured isomorphism.** For `R⊆X×Y`, functional uniqueness produces `f:pr_X(R)→pr_Y(R)`. Reverse functional uniqueness makes the inverse graph a function, so `f` is a bijection of projection supports. Full-domain totality requires both projections onto their declared domains. If `R` is a subgroup of `G×H`, both projections are homomorphisms; a bijective graph map is a group isomorphism of the projection subgroups. Replace subgroup with linear subspace to obtain a linear isomorphism. If `X,Y` are compact Hausdorff and `R` closed, both projection supports are compact Hausdorff and the graph projections are continuous; a bijective projection from the compact graph onto a Hausdorff support is a homeomorphism. For an n-ary relation, the domain/output grouping must be fixed first. Projecting an already functional graph can destroy functionality; the II-A theorem expressly does not infer it automatically.

**(3) Equivariance.** Suppose a group `G` acts diagonally on `X=∏X_i` and on `D=∏D_i`, with `π_i(gx_i)=gπ_i(x_i)`. Then `gπ(R)=π(gR)=π(R)` for a `G`-invariant parent R. No independently acting, non-equivariant descendant action is implied.

**(4) Scalar action, variations and Hessian.** A scalar `S:X→ℝ` factors as `S=\bar S∘q` with `q:X→D` surjective iff S is constant on q-fibres; this is set-theoretic factorization. Continuity/smoothness/variation requires the admitted quotient and a `C²` chain-rule chart, not merely the factorization. For `f:M→N`, `D²(S∘f)[u,v]=D²S[df(u),df(v)]+dS[D²f(u,v)]`; the second term vanishes if `f` affine or `dS=0` at the target point. A locally stationary eliminated variable y with invertible `H_yy` gives `H_red=H_xx−H_xy H_yy⁻¹ H_yx` by differentiating `∂_y S=0`. For singular elimination the II-F lemma states the compatibility/quotient guard. Stationarity, action occurrence and boundary conditions are not created by applying the matrix formula.

**(5) Operator graph.** For surjective linear `q:H→D`, `\bar A(qv):=q(Av)` is independent of representative iff `A(ker q)⊆ker q`; totality then follows from surjectivity of q. For orthogonal P and Q=I−P, restriction of A to ran(P) requires `QAP=0`, whereas quotient descent through P requires `PAQ=0`. For self-adjoint A these agree with `[A,P]=0`. Without them, `PAP` is merely compression and is not the inherited A-action. The proof is in Paper II-F, not inferred from an untyped isomorphism.

**(6) Chronology.** The generated relation must be the image of an already licensed constructor (or arise from an independently certified later constructor) before the claimed use. A bijection, common host, same cardinality, or availability of Hom is a mathematical fact, not an `Orig_Σ` certificate. This corollary is a legitimate firewall, not a new GP.

Tests exercise image/pullback/saturation, non-total two-sided graphs, loss of functionality on quotient projection, n-ary port grouping, linear and group graphs, diagonal equivariance, action fibre constancy, nonlinear Hessian term, regular Schur response, singular block compatibility, quotient vs restriction, self-adjoint commutation, and chronology non-inference.

**Adversarial examples that invalidate stronger, unclaimed shortcuts, not the theorem:**
- A graph `{(0,0),(1,1)}` is functional, but identifying both input representatives sends it to `{(0,0),(0,1)}`, **not** a functional descendant map. The theorem requires an additional functionality test.
- The unsaturated subset `{0}` under `q(x)=x mod 2` has saturation hull `{0,2}`; the theorem explicitly forbids reverse predicate descent in this case.
- For `P=diag(1,0)`, `A=[[1,1],[0,2]]` preserves ran P (`QAP=0`) but does not descend through P (`PAQ≠0`), whereas `A=[[1,0],[1,2]]` descends but does not preserve ran P. These are exact witnesses to the distinction proved by Paper II-F.
- With `S(z)=z²+3z` and `f(t)=t²` at t=1, the true second derivative is 18. Omitting the `dS·D²f` term gives 8; thus the source's term is essential.

No G0/GP2-admissible countermodel to the theorem's conditional scope was produced. The controls validate mathematics; they do not prove independent licensing or physical occurrence of any particular parent relation or action.

## 84. E3 independent verification

**Constructor-word order.** On the frozen free typed-word category, a composable pair `w₂∘w₁` concatenates its lists of licensed constructors. Therefore `ρ(w₂∘w₁)=ρ(w₁)+ρ(w₂)`. This concerns formal word provenance, not a minimal word representing the same endomorphism.

**Parity.** For a multiplicative character `χ(w)∈{±1}`, define `χ(w)=(-1)^{b(w)}`. Then `χ(w₂∘w₁)=χ(w₂)χ(w₁)` implies `b(w₂∘w₁)=b(w₁)+b(w₂) mod 2`. No sign is assigned where the source says a parity entry is undefined.

**Clifford filtration.** For orthonormal generators `e₁,e₂`, `e_i²=1` and `e₁e₂+e₂e₁=0`. A product of raw degree 2 can reduce to a scalar (degree 0); the Clifford relations preserve degree modulo 2. This is a filtration with an exact parity residue.

**Round S³ scalar harmonics.** In ambient ℝ⁴ let `r²=x₁²+x₂²+x₃²+x₄²`; the quadratic `h₂=x₁²−r²/4` is harmonic, and `x₁²=h₂+r²/4`. Cubically, `p₃=x₁(x₁²−x₂²)` has Laplacian `4x₁`; `h₃=p₃−(r²x₁)/3` has Laplacian zero since `Δ(r²x₁)=12x₁`. Thus `p₃=h₃+(r²x₁)/3`. Restricting to `r²=R²` demonstrates even drops `2→0` and `3→1`, and exact antipodal parity. This checks the scalar/round-sphere domain, not unspecified spinorial harmonic operations.

**Derived series.** For a *fully balanced* word defined by `δ₀(g)=g`, `δ_{d+1}=[δ_d(first half),δ_d(second half)]`, the number of primitive leaves is `L₀=1`, `L_{d+1}=2L_d`, so `L_d=2^d`. The verbal subgroup `D^d(H)` can contain products of such words; the theorem does not assign a unique tree to every subgroup element.

**Path provenance.** If `R_D=π(R_P)` merges source paths, provenance can be retained as the generated origin-word **witness** `(P,w,endpoint)` because the frozen constructor-origin certificate and its licensed composition remain available. A bare descendant relation or endpoint cannot generally recover a unique signature. This is the only mathematically warranted reading of “transports the complete path signature”; the theorem's next remark explicitly makes the same distinction. A stronger endpoint-invariant reading would be false, but is *not* the source-as-qualified claim.

## 85. E4 adversarial tests and exact computations

**Reproducible script:** `Proofs/Paper2A-GradedFilteredPCADBatch14-v1_1-verify.py`; results `Proofs/Paper2A-GradedFilteredPCADBatch14-v1_1-results.json`. It passed **31/31 exact symbolic/finite controls**. One initial script typo in a local `S₄` commutator enumeration was corrected before the final run; it was a test-harness error, not a mathematical issue in the source.

- Quaternion `Q₈`: `i²=(-1)` and `i⁻¹j⁻¹ij=(-1)` exactly, with different displayed primitive-word lengths (2 versus 4). No candidate-independent endpoint degree follows.
- `S₄`: commutator values form `A₄` (12 elements); their commutators form `V₄` (4 elements); the next derived subgroup is trivial. The explicit depth-two balanced four-leaf commutator values equal the four-element second derived subgroup in this test group. This checks the syntactic tree count and group derived-series convention, not a universal minimal-length bound.
- PCAD projection: `R={(a,0),(b,1)}` projects to `{(*,0),(*,1)}` after identifying `a,b`; the image need not be functional on the common descended input. An unsaturated predicate also does not descend via reverse pullback. Those facts are exactly what PCAD separately requires; this is not an admissible GP2 counterexample to a claimed endpoint-invariant signature.
- Exact Clifford Pauli-matrix and S³ harmonic/Fischer decompositions, including the antipodal parity signs, pass.

No additional physical license, whole-parent Lie certificate, branch selection, lower bound on intrinsic group word length, or unique endpoint signature follows from these mathematical controls.

# Batch 15 — Conditional quadratic support, exact subgroup sampling, and physical-role boundary

**Audit date:** 8 November 2026. **Current source:** `Paper2A-GeometricConstitution-v3_206.tex`, framework `Paper00-PapersMinimal-v2_358(1).zip`. **Imported geometry:** `Paper1B-M8Geometry-v1_128.tex` and `Paper2F-TechnicalMaterial-v1_57.tex`. **DAG comparison baseline:** `Paper00-DAGAtlas-v1_29.zip`. **Protocol:** proof-source priority, direct import replay, unique condition nodes, and per-route E1/E3/E4 evidence. This is a partial claim audit, not a universal source rewrite or validation of the entire historical theorem graph.

## 89. Exact target and quantifiers

Theorem `p2a:thm:v233-quadratic-support-discharge`, II-A **9055–9085**, is a conditional statement: an **already occurring** role with a **licensed finite constructor word on occurring roots** has three frozen requirements:

1. exact evaluation of **all** scalar inner products on `\(\mathcal P_2=E_0\oplus E_1\oplus E_2\)`;
2. a **finite, multiplication-closed point support within the already constituted** `SU(2)` parent;
3. **subgroup restriction as a GP3 comparison**, demonstrably faithful to *all other frozen content of the same role*.

Under **all three**, the finite group candidates are, up to conjugacy, the binary tetrahedral, octahedral, and icosahedral groups, and the unique unmarked least **group type** is `\([2T]\)`:

\[
\mathfrak C_{\mathcal P_2}^{\rm grp}=\{[2T],[2O],[2I]\},\qquad
[2T]\preccurlyeq_{\rm subgrp}[2O],\quad
[2T]\preccurlyeq_{\rm subgrp}[2I].
\]

The conclusion is a **generated orbit-level role output** (co-realized in the existing occurrence), not a chosen embedded subgroup, labelled vertex set, frame, trimer, instrument, physical weighting, or autonomous support-token occurrence. Source lines **9082–9085** make that explicit. Not even an *abstract* `SL(2,3)\cong2T` identification alone yields an embedded momentum support (typed finite-output atlas, II-A **9015–9029**).

**Adjudication:** `VERIFIED-CONDITIONAL`, with no new proof gap in the declared role domain. No inference from GP1 finite point resolution alone to requirement (ii), nor from the native momentum seed alone to requirement (i), was found or asserted by the source.

Uniform averaging gives an exact mathematical rule; cyclic and binary dihedral are excluded, and `2T` is least in subgroup order. | EARLIER PROVED, E3 independently replayed |

**Important differentiation:** the classification of finite subgroup **types** is mathematical; GP3 makes it a same-physical-role leastness statement **only when condition (iii) is genuinely satisfied**. The II-A theorem does not assume a physically uniform sampling instrument: I-B shows an exact *uniform mathematical alternative* exists by averaging weights, not that averaging the measured instrument has occurred.

## 91. Independent mathematical derivation (E3)

### A. Complete quadratic scalar Gram means a four-design

Write `\(\mathcal P_2=E_0\oplus E_1\oplus E_2\)` on the round `S^3`, with real dimensions `1+4+9=14`. Products `\(\overline f g\)` of `\(f,g\in\mathcal P_2\)` span the restrictions of all polynomials of total degree at most four, so matching their Haar pairings is equivalent to exact cubature through degree four. In particular a positive point-evaluation Gram representation has at least 14 nonzero nodes. If a finite group support carries *any* exact normalized point-weight rule, average its translates over the group; Haar invariance makes the averaged, **uniform** finite-group rule exact. This is a mathematical existence reduction, not a physical weight-selection theorem.

### B. Nonexceptional finite subgroups are excluded without assuming an axis-free predicate

A cyclic subgroup lies in a real two-plane, so some nonzero degree-one linear form vanishes on every point but has positive Haar norm. A binary-dihedral subgroup preserves an unoriented imaginary-quaternion axis `\(u\)` under conjugation. The restricted quadratic harmonic
\[
h_u(q)=\langle\operatorname{Ad}_q u,u\rangle\in E_2
\]
obeys `\(h_u(q)^2=1\)` on the whole subgroup, while `\(\int_{S^3}h_u(q)^2\,d\sigma=1/3\)`. Hence **no normalized weights** on such a subgroup can give all `\(\mathcal P_2\)` inner products. The finite-subgroup classification then leaves only `2T`, `2O`, `2I`. Their design strengths make each sufficient. These arguments derive the noncyclic exceptional range and the common central element `-1` from the declared subgroup/Gram conditions, not as extra assumptions.

### C. Leastness and what restriction preserves

Every embedded `2O` or `2I` contains a `2T` subgroup, whereas `2T` is itself exact through degree four. On the exact *band images*, the map
\[
R=\sqrt{|\Gamma|/|\Gamma'|}\,\operatorname{res}_{\Gamma'}\big|_{S_{\Gamma,2}\mathcal P_2}
\]
intertwines the two normalized sampling maps and is unitary (both are isometries of `\(\mathcal P_2\)`). Thus **if the role freezes only compatible exact-band and surviving group-law content**, the subgroup comparison is admissible and GP3 yields `\([2T]\)` uniquely. No map between the **full** finite point Hilbert spaces is thereby proved, and restriction is not claimed to preserve a larger parent's full left-regular action or arbitrary marked point tests. This is exactly why condition (iii) is essential.

### D. Native metric–Gram constructor is a separate conditional route

II-A `p2a:thm:v236-qop-exceptional-frontier`, **9100–9139**, concerns an already occurring finite operation role `\(\mathsf{QOp}_m\)` for `m≥2`. Its nonempty finite multiplication-closed subset `Z` is a subgroup (powers eventually repeat); its retained content is the group law and the exact sampled complete band, **not** individual node tests or full point permutations. In that narrower role I-B's comparisons yield

\[
\operatorname{Irr}(\mathsf{QOp}_m)=
\begin{cases}
\{[2T]\},&m=2,\\
\{[2O],[2I]\},&m=3\quad\text{(incomparable)},\\
\{[2I]\},&m=4,5,\\
\varnothing,&m\ge6.
\end{cases}
\]

II-A `p2a:cor:v236-native-qop2`, **9141–9159**, supplies a *conditional bridge* from retained `E_1` **if** the same occurring operation role licenses the function-valued pointwise metric–Gram constructor: for normalized unit-coordinate restrictions `q_a` on `S^3(R)`,
\[
R^2\langle\nabla q_a,\nabla q_b\rangle=\delta_{ab}-q_aq_b,
\qquad\operatorname{Sym}^2E_1=E_0\oplus E_2.
\]
It does not derive that operation from generic scalar momentum seed grammar. II-A `p2a:rem:v233-native-constructor-boundary`, **9535–9557**, explicitly says the native seed does **not** license unrestricted product/tensor generation. This means there is no source contradiction between smooth mathematical access to quadratic functions and a separate physical constructor license for using those functions as tests.

## 92. E4 independent exact arithmetic and adversarial boundaries

**Script:** `Proofs/Paper2A-QuadraticSupportBatch15-v1_1-verify.py`. **Results:** `Proofs/Paper2A-QuadraticSupportBatch15-v1_1-results.json`. The exact `\(\mathbb Q(\sqrt5)\)` rational arithmetic verifier passed **26/26** controls. Model supports use coordinate-squared patterns: `2T` comprises 8 axes and 16 Hurwitz half-units; `2O` adds 24 two-coordinate half-diagonal units; `2I` adds 96 even-permutation golden-ratio units. Odd moments vanish by sign symmetry. It checks **every even-coordinate monomial** through design degrees 5, 7, 11, respectively; symbolic Haar even moments use
\[
\int_{S^3}\prod_{i=0}^3q_i^{2a_i}\,d\sigma
=\frac{\prod_i(2a_i)!}{4^{\sum_i a_i}(\sum_i a_i+1)!\prod_i a_i!}.
\]

The sharp first failures exactly match I-B:

| Group | Design degree | Source/test witness | Group average | Haar average | Difference |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `2T` | 5 | `q_0^6` | `3/32` | `5/64` | `1/64` |
| `2O` | 7 | `q_0^8` | `15/256` | `7/128` | `1/256` |
| `2I` | 11 | `q_0^12` | `133/4096` | `33/1024` | `1/4096` |

The same script independently checks: cardinalities 24/48/120; containment of standard `2T` coordinate support in both larger supports; full quadratic Gram moments `\(\int q_0^4=1/8\)` and `\(\int q_0^2q_1^2=1/24\)`; `\(\mathcal P_2\)` dimension 14; `m=3` subgroup incomparability (`48\nmid120`); and the uniform-support sixth-order discriminator `\(\mathcal A^{\rm supp}_6(2T)=1/64\)` versus zero for `2O,2I`.

**Deliberate negative control:** I-B `p1b:prop:v233-hopf72`, **17184–17228**, constructs a 72-node **uniform antipodal 5-design** with an identity anchor after recentering but **no group closure and no Hurwitz 24-cell**. The two radial nodes `\(u_\pm=(1\pm1/\sqrt3)/2\)` integrate `u^0,u^1,u^2,u^3` exactly; a six-angle grid integrates the angular degrees. This meets the **quadratic sampling** condition and much else, but not the group-law condition, so it is **not** a counterexample to the theorem. It refutes only an illicit unconditional shortcut from finite exact Gram/antipodality to `2T` support.

The sixth-moment discriminator is also a **different observable**: `\(\mathcal A^{\rm supp}_6>0\)` is equivalent to the `2T` type *within already occurring exact quadratic subgroup supports*, but it cannot be deduced from their common degree-four moments. Obtaining that number as a *physical* discriminator requires a licensed uniform-support sixth-moment readout, not merely a mathematical calculation (II-A **9506–9532** and I-B **5178–5220**).

# Batch 16 — Intrinsic group-word restriction, descent and finite derived-series output

**Target:** `p2a:thm:v188-group-word-restriction` (Paper II-A v3.206, lines 10456–10495), `p2a:rem:v188-finite-support-occurrence-firewall` (10497–10505). **Current framework:** `Paper00-PapersMinimal-v2_358`; source frozen in `Paper00-DAGAtlas-v1_30/Sources-v2_358/`. **Level:** E1 replay + independent E3 and E4 42 exact group controls. No physical-occurrence certificate is claimed.

## 96. BND-B16-001 — exact target boundary

- **[CONST]:** a saturated parent owns a constituted group `G` (II-A 10422–10435, 10456–10459).
- **[GEN]:** the subgroup support `H ≤ G` is already generated, before restriction; `w:G^n→G` is already retained and is a finite word in multiplication, inversion and the identity in the owner's licensed grammar `Σ_P` (II-A 10458–10470). A bare abstract subgroup or mathematically writable word does not satisfy the physical premise.
- **[DER]:** `w|_{H^n}` has codomain `H` exactly because `H` is closed under group multiplication and inverse. The explicit word-closure hypothesis is automatically true for group words in a subgroup, not an additional selection rule.
- **[GEN, QUOTIENT]:** for quotient `H/Z`, the central normal `Z` must be a generated output and the quotient target licensed. Algebraic representative-independence holds modulo `Z`, not necessarily as equality in `H` (10472–10474, 10486–10488).
- **[GEN, FINITE-PREFIX]:** each requested derived-series step requires its **own licensed subgroup-generation and output-retention** certificate. A finite word can be evaluated without thereby constituting all possible closures or all infinite descendants (10475–10479, 10488–10494).
- **[HOST]:** the finite-set termination estimate is a mathematical statement about finite `H`; no infinite stabilization is implied merely by finite presentation.
- **[REAL]:** generated descendants remain same-owner typed role content, not an independently occurring factor, point carrier, instrument, or cross-descendant action (10494, 10497–10505). The source expressly blocks use of a Cartan graph to assert finite position-carrier occurrence.
- **[MIN/CAL]:** no new GP3 minimum or dimensionful calibration is selected.

## 97. E1 — exact proof replay

1. If `H ≤ G`, any term of the group-word grammar `{1, ·, inverse}` evaluated on `H` lies in `H`, by structural induction on the word. The ambient and restricted evaluation agree identically on `H^n`. No redefined group operation is introduced. This establishes the first boxed conclusion (10460–10470, proof 10482–10486).
2. Let `q:H→H/Z` be the quotient homomorphism for the generated normal subgroup `Z`. Structural induction yields `q(w_H(h_1,...,h_n)) = w_{H/Z}(q(h_1),...,q(h_n))`; hence the factor map is well-defined modulo `Z`. Crucially, this does not imply equality of parent **values** for representatives in the same coset (10472–10474, 10486–10488).
3. `[H,H]` is **the subgroup generated by the image of the commutator word**, not generally the raw commutator image itself; the theorem explicitly cites licensed subgroup closure. Apply the same argument to each retained successor group to obtain any requested finite prefix, subject to all licenses (10475–10476, 10488–10490).
4. If `H` is finite, a strict descendant `D^{j+1}(H) < D^j(H)` has index at least two; iterating `|D^{j+1}| ≤ |D^j|/2` proves at most `⌊log₂|H|⌋` strict decreases. If `D^{j+1}=D^j`, all later derivatives are equal, and the stable group may be nontrivial and perfect (10477–10479, 10490–10494).
5. No passage in these four proofs identifies the abstractly generated subgroup with an independently occurring point set or transports its group word to a second physical factor without the parent relation and a new typed descent certificate (II-A 10497–10505; PCAD 5674–5734; II-A 9535–9557).

## 98. E3/E4 — independent exact controls

`Proofs/Paper2A-GroupWordRestrictionBatch16-v1_1-verify.py` writes the exact control record `Proofs/Paper2A-GroupWordRestrictionBatch16-v1_1-results.json`. The current run passed **42/42** mathematical controls. The calculation is independent of Paper II-A's proof step: it constructs finite permutation groups and quaternion groups directly from multiplication tables, computes closures, quotient cosets, and commutator-derived series.

- S4: successive derived orders `24→12→4→1`, with `S4'=A4`, `A4'=V4`. H=point-stabilizing S3 is a non-normal subgroup and still correctly inherits all group words. Thus normality of H is **not** necessary for restriction.
- A5: `A5'=A5`, a nontrivial perfect fixed point. Thus finite does **not** mean the derived series reaches the identity.
- Exact quaternion groups over `Q(√2)`: enumerated 24-element `2T` and 48-element `2O`, checked full multiplication closure and commutators, and obtained `2O→2T→Q8→{±1}→1`. Independently supports Paper I-A `p1:prop:exceptional-abelianizations` (5626–5666) and its Paper II-E consumer (1115–1150).
- Q8 center: `i` and `−i` are identical cosets but `i^{-1}≠(−i)^{-1}` in the parent. Their **quotient** inverses agree, proving that representative-independence must be understood modulo Z.
- An S3 non-normal subgroup cannot be used as a normal quotient: coset multiplication is ambiguous. A three-element subset not closed under multiplication fails commutator-word restriction. Both are invalid **countermodels** to the source theorem because they violate its hypothesis.
- The noncommutative Cartan graph `g↦(g,g^{-1})` is not generally a homomorphism into `G×G` with componentwise group law. The source uses it only as a descendant **relation**, not as a group morphism or newly occurring position carrier.
- An infinite finitely presented free group `F2` has nonstabilizing derived series: its commutator subgroup is a nonabelian free group of countably infinite rank (Nielsen–Schreier); iterating leaves each derivative nonabelian and proper in its predecessor. This proves the source's warning that finite **presentation** does not imply finite derived-series stabilization. This last example is a mathematical argument, not an executable physical model in the 42 finite controls.

Promotion requires a frozen licensed typed constructor that explicitly names that output target. Equal dimension, isomorphic representations, common scalar support or common parent ancestry cannot substitute. - **[GEN/DER] Generated-process projectability (10520–10537).** `F_t:X→X` and `pi:X→Y` are ALREADY generated. Define `Y0=im(pi)`. The factor map is unique ON `Y0` provided every `pi F_t` is fibre-constant. The target `Y`, continuity/smoothness and physical target retention are distinct concerns. Finite state example imports `p2a:prop:v257-observable-process-target` (3885–3918).

An exact new formula for that SAME descendant reclassifies it mathematically; uniqueness, gluing or positivity of an arbitrary boundary functional cannot construct a second physically occurring action. ## 102. E1: projectability proof replay, both directions

Let `x ~ x'` mean `pi(x)=pi(x')`. If a map `barF_t:Y0→Y0` satisfies `barF_t pi=pi F_t`, then for `x~x'` the outputs `pi F_t x` and `pi F_t x'` agree (NECESSITY). Conversely, if these outputs agree for every fibre, define `barF_t(pi x)=pi F_t x`. The value is independent of representative and lies in `Y0`; since `pi:X→Y0` is onto by definition, the factor is UNIQUE (SUFFICIENCY). Thus:

`(exists unique barF_t:Y0→Y0, pi F_t=barF_t pi) iff (pi F_t is constant on all pi-fibres)`.

If `F_{t+s}=F_t F_s`, then `barF_{t+s} pi=pi F_{t+s}=pi F_t F_s=barF_t barF_s pi`; surjectivity onto `Y0` proves the semigroup law. If `F_0=id`, its identity descends. If `pi` is not onto all of `Y`, the factor equation imposes NO values on `Y\Y0` and cannot determine a unique full-`Y` map; it does not rule out arbitrary extensions. All claims agree exactly with lines 10520–10532.

**Regularity:** A continuous surjective quotient `pi:X→Y0` and continuous `F_t` give continuous `barF_t`; an arbitrary continuous surjection with an incompatible codomain topology need not. If `X` compact and `Y0` Hausdorff, continuous onto maps are closed and hence quotient. Smoothness needs a smooth-quotient or a separate descent regularity certificate. No source assertion promotes a set-theoretic factor to automatic physical-time joint continuity or differentiability on an arbitrary declared readout codomain.

**Concrete finite state target:** By `p2a:prop:v257-observable-process-target`, the restriction `q:S(A)→S(B*)`, `q(omega)=omega|B*`, is SURJECTIVE when `B*` is a retained invariant finite-dimensional C* subalgebra and the closure/evaluation/target are licensed. For `F_t(omega)=omega∘T_t`, invariance gives `q F_t=barF_t q` with `barF_t(eta)(b)=eta(T_t b)`. Finite-dimensional state-space topology makes the affine descended semigroup continuous on all `S(B*)`. This imports the prior verified Batch 8 theorem, not a new physics occurrence assumption.

## 103. E3: independent mathematical construction and exact example

A factorization through `pi` is equivalent to constant values on the equivalence classes of its *kernel pair*: this is the elementary universal property of a set quotient, independent of the source's representative-based argument. For a continuous-time stochastic example, take the partition matrix

`V=[[1,0],[1,0],[0,1],[0,1]]`,

and row-probability generators

`Q=[[-1,0,1,0],[0,-1,0,1],[2,0,-2,0],[0,2,0,-2]]`,

`Qbar=[[-1,1],[2,-2]]`.

Then `Q V=V Qbar` exactly, implying `exp(tQ)V=V exp(tQbar)` for all `t>=0` by power-series commutation. Every two-block probability state has a four-state extension. This is an independent exact mathematical model of the prior C* state-restriction result, **not** a claimed born–Hopf realization of that particular Markov generator.

For boundary reclassification, the action `S(x,z)=(3x^2+2xz+2z^2)/2` has stationary eliminated variable `z=-x/2` and descendant `S_eff(x)=5x^2/4`; Schur reduction independently gives `H_eff=3-(1)(2^{-1})(1)=5/2`. Both describe the SAME descendant. An actual positive Gaussian integral, if licensed, has a separate determinant/measure prefactor (Paper I-B 16719–16747), so merely observing a mathematical Schur or gluing identity does not create another action owner.

## 104. E4: negation attacks and adversarial domain controls

`Proofs/Paper2A-ProcessProjectabilityBatch17-v1_1-verify.py` with exact results `Proofs/Paper2A-ProcessProjectabilityBatch17-v1_1-results.json` passed **30/30** independently executable exact controls, including symbolic matrix exponentials.

1. **Fibre failure:** on `X={0,1}^2`, `pi(a,b)=a`, `F(a,b)=(b,a)`, the visible next value depends on hidden `b`; no descended map. In contrast `F(a,b)=(1-a,a XOR b)` descends to a visible flip with iterated semigroup identities. Hence fibre constancy is genuinely necessary.
2. **Incomplete image:** if `Y={0,1,2}` but `pi(X)={0,1}`, two different semigroup-compatible extensions disagree at `2` while both satisfy the factor equation. The source never claims uniqueness there.
3. **Topology warning (OUTSIDE compact-Hausdorff physics case):** continuous onto identity `pi` from a discrete three-point `X` to `Y` with topology `{empty,{0},Y}` need not be quotient. The continuous original transposition `(0 1)` descends setwise but is not continuous on `Y`. This is a mathematical boundary test, NOT an admissible physical model or refutation.
4. **Smoothness warning (host mathematics):** for `pi(x)=x^3` and smooth translations `F_t(x)=x+t`, the descended `barF_t(y)=(cuberoot(y)+t)^3` is continuous but fails `C^1` at `y=0` for `t>0`. A topological quotient is not sufficient for smooth descent.
5. **Non-lumpable finite process:** change just one hidden state's block exit rate in `Q` above. The two rows of `QV` in the same visible block disagree, so no aggregate generator satisfying `QV=V Qbar` exists. The original source correctly treats such processes as nonprojectable.
6. **Action owner:** positivity, regularity, Gaussian normalization or canonical form do not entail licensing of a new action/field target; the source protects this distinction explicitly.

## 108. E3 — Independent algebraic strengthening (not a source theorem)

For a unital typed action of coordinate algebra `S` on role module `V` and fixed `P!=0`, define its annihilator

`Ann(P)={s in S : sP=0}`.

Then `q=sP!=0` iff `s not in Ann(P)`. Normalization of `P` is irrelevant to this logical distinction. For `S=C` with ordinary scalar multiplication, `Ann(P)={0}`, yet one still needs a proof/occurrence of `s!=0` to infer `q!=0`. For operator-valued `s`, a nonzero coordinate can lie in `Ann(P)`: with `P=e1` and `s=E12`, `E12 e1=0` even though `E12!=0`. This is **stronger caution** for typed coordinates than a scalar formula. If the action map `s->sP` is injective, `s!=0` suffices for `q!=0`, but normalization alone does not produce such injectivity or `s!=0`.

A separate independently supplied full-rank member `D=[[0,T],[T†,0]]` with `T` unitary has `D²=I` and is nonzero. It is a toy algebraic witness of how a *different premise* supplies nonvanishing; it is NOT a claim that the Born–Hopf rooted physical constructor has been newly derived by these finite calculations.

Finite dimensionality and the isomorphisms of the source/target spaces imply equal rank, equal kernel/cokernel dimensions. In the square case `det(T')=det(L)det(T)det(R)`, and invertibility of the factors preserves whether the determinant vanishes. This establishes the exact claim.

The corollary lists potential *generated defect-changing constructors*; it does not establish which one actually occurred or license a new event from observing an unexplained change. Uniform positive lower singular bounds on the two factors **AND** positive liminf source gap yield uniform positive descended gap. Pointwise invertibility or pointwise full rank cannot replace the liminf input for this transport-based guarantee.

## 113. E3 — independent mathematical proofs and non-implications

### Finite two-sided transport

For finite complex Hilbert spaces and invertible `L,R`, multiplication by `R` is a bijection of the domain and multiplication by `L` a bijection of the codomain. Thus:

`ker(LTR)=R^{-1} ker(T)`, `im(LTR)=L(im T)`, `rank(LTR)=rank(T)`, `dim coker(LTR)=dim E'_L-rank T=dim E_L-rank T`.

The defect pair is a complete *zero-mode count invariant* under this transport; it is **not** an invariant under arbitrary noninvertible projection, Schur complement, or independent source addition.

### Smallest-singular-value inequality

Let `s_min(A)` denote the smallest singular value in the conventional `min(dim source,dim target)` singular-value list. For invertible `L,R`, min-max singular-value inequalities give, for every corresponding index `j`,

`σ_j(LTR) ≥ σ_min(L) σ_j(TR) ≥ σ_min(L) σ_j(T) σ_min(R)`.

The endpoint case `j=min(dim E_L,dim E_R)` yields the displayed theorem. Alternatively use the positive-definite Loewner bounds `L* L ≥ σ_min(L)^2 I` and `R R* ≥ σ_min(R)^2 I` and the positive singular spectra. For rank-deficient square `T`, the ordinary `σ_min(T)` is zero, so the inequality is correct but gives a vacuous zero lower bound. A gap *above the zero modes* would instead require an explicitly defined `σ_min^+(T)`; source terminology is not to be silently changed. Under invertible factors the corresponding nonzero singular spectrum also satisfies an analogous bound, but that is an independent strengthening, not the source's claim.

For a scale family `T_s'=L_s T_s R_s`, if `inf_s σ_min(L_s) ≥ l>0`, `inf_s σ_min(R_s) ≥ r>0`, and `liminf_{s→IR}σ_min(T_s)≥g>0`, then `liminf σ_min(T_s')≥lgr>0`. No convergence of `T_s'` to an actual endpoint operator, source-to-pole matching, or IR state existence follows from this inequality alone. If one reads “requires” in the source as an absolute necessary condition on *all possible* transported terminal gaps, an additional boundedness/non-singular-terminal hypothesis matters: `T_s=s I`, `L_s=s^{-1}I`, `R_s=I` gives `T_s'=I` despite `σ_min(T_s)→0`, but `L_s` is singular/divergent at the terminal endpoint and falls outside the claimed **undeformed nonsingular terminal** scope. The example is a semantic stress test, not an in-domain physical counterexample or a new certified gap.

### UV kernel and IR boundary

When `D_UV X0=u`, the full UV solution set is `X0+K`, `K=ker D_UV`. The joint boundary equation reduces to `B_IR(k)=b-B_IR(X0)` on `k∈K`. Therefore:

- **Existence iff** `b-B_IR(X0)∈B_IR(K)`;
- **Uniqueness if it exists iff** `B_IR|_K` is injective; and
- Under injectivity, the joint problem has **at most one** solution, exactly as stated.

Existence and injectivity are logically distinct. This sharpened classification is an independent mathematical addition for clarity, not an alleged missing physical constructor.

Numerical stress tests corroborate but do not prove the inequality; the E3 argument is the proof.

Controls also deliberately show (i) finite full rank can coexist with zero terminal liminf gap; (ii) injective IR restriction is insufficient for existence; (iii) a positive excitation gap above a nontrivial kernel is distinct from positive ordinary smallest singular value. ### 119. E3 — precise mathematical analysis, failure cases and sharpness

**Finite noncommuting rectangular expansion.** If the typed maps `E_aT_a:H→Q` share a common domain and `K_Q:Q→Q` is invertible, multiplication and adjunction give

`(Σ_a E_aT_a)† K_Q^{-1}(Σ_b E_bT_b) = Σ_{a,b}T_a† E_a† K_Q^{-1}E_b T_b`.

For Hermitian positive `K_Q`, the full return is Hermitian nonnegative. The ordered `(a,b)` and `(b,a)` cross terms are adjoints; neither can generally be deleted separately. In the source, `B†K_Q^{-1}B` denotes the **quadratic return**. The overall sign and numerical prefactor of a *fully integrated on-shell action* depend on its prior declared action convention; they are not determined from the presence of `B` alone. The theorem is valid as a retained named quadratic word, not as a universal formula for arbitrary nonlinear receiver elimination.

**Countermodel to premature norm readout.** Let `Q=C`, `K_Q=1`, `E_1=E_2=1`, `q(z)=|z|`. The two tuples `(T_1,T_2)=(1,1)` and `(1,-1)` have equal separate terminal data `(1,1)`, while the complete returns are `|1+1|²=4` and `|1-1|²=0`. Hence **no** function on the scalar pair `(q(T_1),q(T_2))` can reproduce this return on a family containing both tuples. This is a mathematical stress test; it is **not** asserted that both tuples are complete physical GP2-admissible candidates in a particular Born–Hopf action.

**Sharp nonimplication.** Noninjectivity of `q` by itself does not force cross-return loss on every claim. If `Q=C²`, `K_Q=I` and `E_1,E_2` are orthogonal coordinate embeddings, then `E_1†K_Q^{-1}E_2=0` and `F=|T_1|²+|T_2|²` factors through the separate norms. Alternatively, if a frozen generated family requires `T_2=T_1`, the complete `F=4|T_1|²` also factors through the norms **on that family**. These are scoped *mathematical sufficiency* examples, not retroactive licenses for a new physical scalar-only constructor.

**Sharp criterion.** Let `X` be the **actual admissible generated** tuple family, `q^×:X→Y` the separate terminal map, and `F:X→Z` the full required value. A unique map `\bar F:q^×(X)→Z` with `F=\bar F∘q^×` exists iff `q^×(x)=q^×(x') ⇒ F(x)=F(x')` for all `x,x'∈X`. For **typed cross relations themselves** to be retained, the applicable source theorem requires their own relation-faithful descent, not merely a coincidentally constant total scalar. The full complex Gram matrix can retain coherent cross terms modulo a common phase while still being a noninjective output; this illustrates an *allowed independently certified sufficient quotient*, not an added GP.

**Domain edge.** If `K_Q` is singular, `K_Q^{-1}` is undefined as displayed. A Moore–Penrose inverse is another licensed word with kernel and range compatibility obligations; it is not identical to a true inverse. Thus the source's inverse expression is read in its **defined inverse** domain. The standard scalar quadratic action `1/2 y²-by` has on-shell value `-b²/2`; this also illustrates why one must not automatically equate the nonnegative quadratic return to an on-shell action without the established sign/factor convention.

## 126. E3 — Independent mathematical replay

Let `N=|X|≥1` and identify the label carrier as `C^(N²)` with orthonormal `e_(x,y)`. Put `e=e_(x0,x0)`. Any linear `A` satisfying `Ae=0` and `Ae_v=e_v` for every other basis vector `v` equals `Q=I-ee*` on an exhaustive basis. Uniqueness does not require self-adjointness or an additional GP3 optimization. The resulting `Q` *is* self-adjoint/idempotent of rank `N²−1`. The specified `Ω=(1/N)Σ_v e_v` has norm one, so `⟨Ω,QΩ⟩=1−|⟨e,Ω⟩|²=1−1/N²`. For `N=5`, the value is `24/25`. The nonuniform rooted vector `e` itself has expectation zero; **the invariant-vector expectation is not universal over arbitrary preparations**. The uniform vector is permutation-invariant under the induced label permutation, but the **rooted projector is not full-group-invariant** unless the root is fixed: it is equivariant only for the root stabilizer in general.

For group `I`, subgroup `H` with index five and coset representatives `g_0,...,g_4`, the 25 *labelled* translates `g_i H g_j^{-1}` count every group element five times: fixing `j`, the five left cosets `g_i H g_j^{-1}` partition the group. Removing `g_0H g_0^{-1}=H` gives

`(1/(25|H|)) Σ_(i,j)≠(0,0) Σ_(q∈g_i H g_j^-1) F(q) = (5/(25|H|)) Σ_(q∈I)F(q) − (1/(25|H|)) Σ_(q∈H)F(q) = τ_I(F) − (1/25)τ_H(F)`;

for `|H|=24`, `|I|=120`, the denominator is 600 and total retained weight is `24/25`. The 25 cell registers **overlap as point sets**, so the identity requires labelled evaluation registers, not disjoint subsets of `I`. Tensoring label deletion with the already retained `C^24` payload gives `rank(Q⊗I_24)=576` inside a 600-dimensional *labelled* carrier; it does not identify that carrier with `C^120` vertices.

The I-B nested Reynolds formula `P_I−P_H/25` uses `H⊂I` and orthogonal inclusions `ran P_I⊂ran P_H`. The three eigenvalues follow on the decomposition `ran P_I⊕(ran P_H⊖ran P_I)⊕ker P_H`: `24/25, −1/25, 0`. This linear-algebra identity is proved; degree-specific ranks at 6 and 12 are imported from the named I-C character calculation, not independently recomputed here.

## 129. Exact statement, sorts, quantifiers and the physical claim

- **Input:** an occurring licensed constructor has already produced native terminal coefficient `m_r` of scalar or role-tagged-coefficient sort, possibly together with retained sibling incidence `rho_r`. This is an **antecedent**, not a fresh proof that a specific mass value or a physical pole exists.
- **Later construction:** `A=A(m_r,rho_r,...)` is formed by a subsequently licensed finite operator constructor, with chronological order frozen **before** evaluating `A`.
- **Forbidden unlicensed inference:** from the existence of `A` and a mathematical spectral functional `s(A)`, do not replace the earlier native generating rule by `m_r := s(A)`, or identify the two as a physical equality, without a separately licensed relation theorem. This applies to eigenvalues, singular values, Takagi values and other spectral functionals.
- **Allowed separate conclusion:** if a legitimate, already licensed theorem proves `m_r=s(A)` on a specified domain, this is a **derived downstream comparison dictionary**. It does not change which constructor generated `m_r`. Physical ownership of source coefficients remains at the earlier terminal operation.
- **Corollary:** Yukawa/CKM/PMNS/mass-matrix/Takagi coordinates can encode previously generated packets. They are not thereby native terminal-mass generators; the correct native flavor claims use the actually generated current/incidence and propagation maps.

**Formal deduction.** Let `w_m` be the frozen physical constructor word generating typed output `m_r`, and let `w_A` be a later licensed word with an input edge from `m_r`. These directed provenance edges cannot be reversed without replacing the fixed grammar. An algebraic spectral map `s` applied to `A` may yield a value equal to `m_r`, but equality of values does not prove equality of generating rules or physical ownership. Postulating a new cross-sort equality without a generated relation would violate the GP2 no-insertion and typed-output clauses; proving it from a licensed relation yields a legitimate derived comparison, not retroactive mass generation. That is exactly the source claim.

**Potentially misleading step that does NOT create a gap.** The printed proof (10934–10937) mentions loss at a noninjective readout; the theorem does not assume the spectral map is always noninjective. Noninjectivity is a **possible mathematical obstruction**, not an indispensable premise of the chronology firewall. Even when `s(A)` is injective on a declared subfamily and `m_r` is algebraically recoverable, its later recovery does not alter the earlier generated provenance. GP2/frozen chronology independently establish the actual theorem.

## 131. E3 sharp mathematical distinction

**Noninjective spectral value.** `A(m)=diag(m,-m)`. For `m=1` or `m=-1`, the singular-value multiset is `{1,1}`. Therefore no universal function of the singular values alone returns the earlier signed `m` on a family containing both configurations. Similarly, the 1×1 complex symmetric Takagi value is `|m|`, which loses phase information.

**Valid comparison equality, not an inverse constructor.** On the already declared `m>0` domain, `A(m)=mI_2` has both eigenvalues and both singular values equal to `m`. If the algebraic relation is licensed for the physical target, the equality is a valid downstream dictionary. It does not transform the spectral operation into the original mass-generation word.

**Flavor/current nonimplication.** Let `H_u=diag(1,2)` and `H_d=diag(3,4)`. For `R` a nontrivial real orthogonal rotation, `H_d'=R H_d R^T` has exactly the same eigenvalues as `H_d`, while its relative eigenframe/current-coordinate matrix changes. The terminal spectra alone do not determine relative weak-current incidence; a separately generated current morphism must be consumed by a native flavor observable claim.

**Source packet compression.** With two terminal role coefficients `(m_1,m_2)`, the later 1×1 incidence sum `R=m_1+m_2` takes the same value on `(1,2)` and `(2,1)`. This shows why role-tagged provenance cannot in general be replaced by a scalar after the fact. The example is a mathematical control, **not** a demonstration that these tuples are both physically GP2-admissible in the framework.

# Batch 23 — Physical-descendant exclusivity and retained bulk scalaron

**Authoritative source:** `Paper2A-GeometricConstitution-v3_206.tex` (SHA-256 `167aec2ef3b1c3f30859ca65cf430778440887466a4451d47e6702af0edb9be0`), framework v2.358. **Coverage:** Corollary `p2a:cor:v3106-physical-descendant-exclusivity` (10962–10993), cosmological remark `p2a:rem:v3106-k16-use` (10995–11003), its one-way successor qualification (11041–11049), named upstream rules and limited VI-A downstream source check. No original paper or global core graph edited.

## 135. Precise theorem, domain and intended observable

One physical action `S_*` exists by hypothesis. A **licensed** boundary/interior constructor admits stationary elimination `q_I=bar q_I(q_partial;y)` with `S_*^partial(q_partial;y)=S_*(q_partial,bar q_I(q_partial;y);y)`. The source **also assumes** a downstream theorem has independently certified `S_*^partial` as the physical descendant consumed in a declared target role. The frozen physical grammar contains **no second owner, carrier or role** reconstituting the eliminated interior as an *autonomous downstream object*. Under exactly those conditions, an independently evolved copy of the eliminated interior cannot be treated as a **coequal downstream physical descendant of the same occurrence**. The original parent remains as derivational data and can remain physical on its independently established bulk-history role.

The targeted physical distinction is **absence of duplicated downstream degrees of freedom**, not a theorem about eliminating all solutions of the parent dynamics, selecting an ordinary-particle owner from Schur reduction alone or altering K16 Cauchy data.

**Citation-domain challenge adjudicated:** the finite-object lemma does not, on its own, apply wholesale to dynamical action variables. The corollary nevertheless already assumes the licensed descendant and no second generated independent role. An extra coequal autonomous child would add an ungenerated role to the same frozen physical presentation, contradicting **GP2 no insertion**. This establishes the negative conclusion at the claim's precise scope without silently extending the specialized lemma. Generated co-consumption ensures the previously licensed reduced relation is not replaced or renormalized. **No new necessary proof gap certified.**

## 137. E3 — Exact stationary reduction and one-way Cauchy distinction

For a finite quadratic action `S(x,z)=a x²/2 + b xz + c z²/2`, with `c≠0` and interior stationary equation `b x+c z=0`, one has

`bar z(x)=-(b/c)x`, `S^partial(x)=1/2(a-b²/c)x²`, `H_eff=a-b²/c`.

This is the original action evaluated on its stationary interior graph and contains the interior response. Adding a new, independently normalized quadratic child would generally change that response and is not an identity derived from the same action. With singular interior Hessian one must retain the constraint relation rather than assert an unconstrained Schur graph.

Crucially, a stationary replacement is **not** an exact replacement for all dynamical histories. For `z¨+z=0` with `z(0)=0`, `z˙(0)=1`, the solution `sin t` continues despite a zero stationary-source solution. The K16 source, Paper VI-A 9112–9132 and 9434–9497, makes the same type distinction: the Schur response uses the same homogeneous scalaron, while its bulk evolution retains inherited Cauchy data. This mathematical analogy is **not** an independent audit of the K16 dynamical construction.

# Batch 24 — Boundary response owner, full stress and fixed-owner cells

**Standalone detailed source/proof report:** `Audits/Paper2A-BoundaryResponseOwnerBatch24-v1_1.md`. The following incorporates its mathematical audit, cross-paper scope boundary and test evidence.

## 140. Exact target and conclusion

The source presupposes an **already occurring** licensed same-action construction whose output is a physical boundary descendant `S_*^∂` on the declared tangent `J_∂`. A downstream theorem independently constitutes a particle carrier `J_P` and supplies an **owner-preserving inclusion/restriction** `ι_P:J_P→J_∂` into the retained response domain. On that antecedent domain, an induced particle response (including licensed Schur, pole or positive-frequency restrictions) is a restriction of the same boundary response. The source explicitly **does not** infer complete boundary-stress or energy-packet identities:

`T_{∂,dyn}^{μν}=T_ord^{μν}`, `Q_∂=Q_ord`.

Such a stronger assertion needs an independently generated **common-owner relation or equivalent typed theorem**. Crucially, that is a *necessary provenance condition*, not by itself a sufficient test of equality of full functional stress: the source's downstream VI-A action-jet certificate separately checks the functional identity.

The adjacent `v3107` remark is **one-way**: no reintroduction of an unlicensed coequal interior child after a physical stationary descendant has been separately certified; the existence of that stationary descendant does not select its particle owner.

The adjacent `v3108` corollary is **also conditional**: if a complete response cell uses the same fixed normalized boundary datum `q`, varying solely through `η(ρ)`; no cell constructor changes `q`'s target sort; and an independent endpoint/common-parent theorem physically types `q` as owner `P`, then every **nonzero** cell member carries that owner. The same generated normalization excludes an extra arbitrary efficiency multiplier *unless separately generated*.
