## 46.2 — Independent mathematics (E3)

**FD/LR logical separation.** The topological group `G` under GP1-TB is compact. LR gives local rectifiable connectivity and in particular local path connectedness; with FD the compact finite-dimensional connected-component structure falls under the locally compact group Lie criterion. In a connected compact Lie group a translation-invariant intrinsic length comparison with finite first-order rates can be modeled by a Carnot–Carathéodory horizontal distribution. Bi-invariance makes the horizontal space an Ad-invariant ideal; bracket-generating forces it to be all of `Lie(G^0)`, so the homogeneous rate is a full invariant norm, not merely a singular sub-Finsler distribution. This explains why both optional hypotheses matter in the imported II-F argument; the source proves quantitative local comparison under its *particular* LR bounds. This independent explanation is not a substitute for its quantified hypotheses.

**John ellipsoid is geometric, not a scale postulate.** For a finite-dimensional norm `F`, the symmetric convex unit ball is bounded with nonempty interior. John's theorem gives one maximal-volume inscribed ellipsoid. Every `F`-isometry maps it to another maximal-volume inscribed ellipsoid, hence fixes it by uniqueness; therefore `Ad(G)` invariance of `F` implies `Ad(G)` invariance of the associated positive quadratic form. The abstract ellipsoid comparison does not choose a physical unit, an independent radius, or an observable `c`.

**GP1 scale and indexed generation depth.** Total boundedness satisfies `N_(X,cd)(ε)=N_(X,d)(ε/c)` for `c>0`. It carries no preferred positive length scale; rescaling even changes distances and rates without violating boundedness. Source `p2:def:generated-boundary-hypothesis` separately registers `P_gbd(q,N0)`, which is a **particular fixed index** of constituted grammar length. For any nested sublevels `Γ^(≤N)`, `P_gbd(q,N0)` implies `∃N` covering support, but the converse fails for the **given** `N0`; it may only certify a larger bound, and no minimum is selected unless a separate argument supplies one.

**Metric curry boundary.** For complete `(X,d)`, `b_z(x)=d(z,x)` gives `||b_x-b_y||_∞=d(x,y)` by triangle inequality and evaluating at `z=x`. This *mathematical identity* does not imply the family is in the generated *physical* B-test grammar. Source `p2:def:metric-partial-evaluation-closure` explicitly imposes that distinct occurrence condition. Selecting a favorite `z` would import a mark not present in the unmarked grammar.

**A1/local versus global.** The Lie algebra of a connected noncommutative compact Lie image has nonzero compact semisimple derived algebra and a root `su(2)`. The bracket of independent vectors in `su(2)≅R^3` leaves their two-plane, so a noncommutative proper two-dimensional subalgebra cannot exist. The homomorphism `SU(2)→L` has a closed connected image but need not be injective; the source separately exactifies the compact globalization `E_adm(su2)=[SU(2)]` by GP2 in its unmarked primitive relation role. Thus neither an arbitrary image nor raw dimension alone asserts a complete GP3 endpoint.

**Unmarked abelian and mixed kernel globalizations.** For `a≅R^k`, every connected compact globalization is `a/Λ` for a full lattice `Λ≅Z^k`; all such groups are abstractly `T^k`, but no distinguished lattice basis remains. In mixed `R×SU(2)`, `Γ_0={(n,(-1)^{εn}):n∈Z}` for `ε∈{0,1}` gives compact groups with trivial pure semisimple kernel; their center profiles differ (product group `U(1)×SU(2)` versus `U(2)`), showing the pure-kernel ban does **not** force unique compact globalization. The source's GP2 proof instead removes extra *pure* kernel via a relation-exact completed lift. Nontrivial pure semisimple quotient `SU(2)→SO(3)` is a mathematical possibility in other signatures, not an unconditional physical GP2 permission or prohibition.

**Endpoint consequence.** The previously repaired candidatewise reachability theorem compares each complete primitive candidate to an exact `U(1)` or `SU(2)` target with *strictness if its skeleton differs*. Therefore an irreducible GP3 candidate cannot have another skeleton; this does not prohibit multiple nonisomorphic *structured* lifts with the same skeleton. The derivation does not revisit or weaken intrinsic `H_1`, dimension 15, or the separately proved momentum `m_p=1`.

**Audited source:** `Paper2A-GeometricConstitution-v3_210.tex`, SHA-256 `2d8a52924317a29031bf143ba3da9e407a42719d1530416dafbcad6f66c876a1` (framework v2.362).  
**Previous source audit:** 236 selected source objects through Batch 46.  
**This batch:** 14 *new* source objects, two previously recorded objects independently upgraded; **250 cumulative selected objects across 47 batches**. The second `\label` on the commutator theorem is an *alias*, not a second theorem.  
**Epistemic result:** E1 source replay + separate E3 mathematical arguments + E4 controls (**66,977/66,977 PASS**); **no new certified in-domain material proof defect**. Existing eight tracked issues remain source-resolved within their previous scopes. No source manuscript changed.

## 47.1 Exact target and first backward proof slice

Primary target: `p2:def:operational-rank-one-factorization` (v3.210 lines 8146–8168) and the single source theorem carrying both labels `p2:thm:gp2-commutator-dichotomy` and `p2:thm:gp2-generated-rank-one-obstruction` (8170–8226).

Let `H_R=ρ_A(⟨R⟩)≤Aut(B(X))`, already an effective *mathematical action image*, with a separately constituted generated test doctrine `B_R` and its action `ρ_R:H_R→Aut(B_R)`. The theorem assumes `ρ_R` **faithful** when it asserts equivalence of noncommutativity and a B-test witness; absence of this test faithfulness does **not** negate the algebraic commutator branch. One-dimensional operational factorization is through a **connected 1D Lie operation group**, not an assertion that Cartan rank is one. The theorem is an in-role *obstruction*, not a global new primitive-role rank selector.

Source dependency chain actually consumed:

`p2:def:geometric-role-existence` (6368–6418) → `p2a:lem:v281-connected-generated-group` (6420–6452) → `p2:thm:geometric-existence-exactification` (6454–~6525) for the primitive algebraic branch → `p2:post:GP2` (2887–3057) for generated-sort faithfulness and B-doctrine occurrence → `p2:def:metric-partial-evaluation-closure` (7392–7412), **only if** choosing canonical profiles as physical tests → `p2:thm:canonical-metric-profile-separation` (7415–~7468) for mathematical profile kernel detection → `p2:thm:gp2-commutator-dichotomy` (8170–8226). `p2:post:GP3` (3732–3814) preserves supplied role distinctions but neither creates nor witnesses a missing test. The endpoint classification `p2:thm:master-compact-role-allocation` is already independently established for its own primitive-role scope and is *not a premise proving* the commutator equivalence.

**Independent proof (E3).** For a group action `ρ` on test objects, nonabelianity is equivalent to some nonidentity commutator `c=[r,s]`. If `ρ` is injective, `ρ(c)≠id`, hence as distinct automorphisms `ρ(c)b≠b` for some `b`. Conversely, `c*b≠b` forces `c≠e`. If an action `ι` factors as `σ∘φ` through a connected 1D Lie group `C`, its Lie algebra has zero bracket and connectedness forces `C` to be abelian; therefore `φ([r,s])=e`. If `ι` is the displayed inclusion, that contradicts `ι([r,s])≠id`. To claim that a **generated physical B-test** witnesses the obstruction additionally requires `ρ_R` faithful; mathematical canonical metric profiles witness it only under the separate metric-curry license. No rank or numerical parameter is introduced.

**Sharp negative control (E4).** `S_3` is nonabelian but its sign action on `X={0,1}` factors through `C_2`, so a nontrivial 3-cycle commutator acts trivially on *every* test of `X`. This violates `ρ_R` faithfulness and therefore does not refute the theorem; it proves the condition is indispensable. By contrast, the regular action on the discrete metric space `X=S_3` with all profiles `b_z(x)=1_{x≠z}` separates the commutator. The latter is a legitimate **mathematical model**, not an automatically occurring physical B-doctrine.

## 47.3 Independent mathematical certificates and critical boundary guards

**Lie globalizations.** If `g` is a finite-dimensional real Lie algebra, its connected Lie integrations are the quotient of its simply connected integration `G~` by a *discrete central* subgroup `K`. In the compact semisimple `g=su(2)` case, `G~=SU(2)` and `Z(G~)={±I}`, leaving only `SU(2)` and `SO(3)` as connected global forms. Both admit finite faithful representations of *their own sorts*; selecting the former from the unmarked source-presentation relation exactness requires GP2's actually constituted no-extra-relation rule, **not** abstract global-form classification. For a noncompact abelian source `R^k`, compact quotients by full lattices are tori and no preferred lattice coordinates arise. Mixed lattice–center compactifications are not globally excluded by the pure semisimple-kernel lemma.

**Torus equivalence and local versus global.** For compact connected **Lie** `G`, any abelian Lie subalgebra lies in a maximal toral Lie algebra, and maximal tori have rank `rank(G)`. Independent commuting `X,Y` exist iff `rank G≥2`. A two-torus subgroup supplies such a pair by differentiation, and a rank≥2 maximal torus includes a closed `T²` factor. Thus the three conditions in `p2:thm:role-local-torus-obstruction` are equivalent for **Lie-certified** parents. The source's `H_R` at this point denotes the post-GP3 endpoint skeleton (defined as such in the surrounding primitive allocation); that is known to be Lie. The same formula applied blindly to an arbitrary earlier compact connected *non-Lie* parent would be ill-typed. This is a necessary domain annotation, **not a certified error in the selected-endpoint theorem**. E4 controls use the cross-product identification `su(2)≅R³`, a rank-two diagonal Cartan of `su(3)`, and product-circle rank addition.

**Group characters and cocharacters.** A continuous `SU(2)→U(1)` homomorphism differentiates to a Lie-algebra homomorphism `su(2)→R`; its differential vanishes because `[su(2),su(2)]=su(2)`, and connectedness forces the whole group map to be trivial. For the reverse map, an abelian compact connected image lies in a maximal torus of `SU(2)`; characters of `U(1)` have integral exponent, so `z↦diag(z^n,z^{-n})`. The center is seen by the parity character `(-1)^{2j}` on the spin `j` irreducible. Every faithful representation of `SU(2)` must include at least one center-odd summand; this is a diagnostic *after* global-form selection, not an independent GP3 center-bit input.

**GP3 existence and no-selector limits.** The present 14-object slice does **not** establish a global well-foundedness theorem for every hypothetical richer-role preorder. In a generic abstract preorder, neither a unique least class nor an irreducible member need exist; using such an *abstract* order as a supposed physical GP3 counterexample would not meet the protocol's actual typed in-domain countermodel requirement. Consequently the filter-order proposition is certified as an order-of-operations rule and the primitive selected branch as an endpoint theorem, not as a universal existence proof for all stronger role grammars. This does not reopen a primitive endpoint or the Paper II-B `m_p=1` theorem.

**BND47:** [CONST] primitive intrinsic order-swap geometry; [REAL] candidatewise exact algebraic operation group; [GEN+REAL] generated B-test doctrine **only when constituted**; [CLASS] abelian/noncommutative branch; [MIN] completed GP3 role-faithful comparisons; [HOST] distance-profile family mathematically separate before metric-curry; [DER] rank-one Cartan and torus exclusion from the **selected Lie skeleton**; [ID/CAL] no automatic scalar clock unit, orientation, Hopf bridge, momentum calibration or level. All physical test claims retain their premise edges.

It checks cyclic-root restrictions of cocharacters for integer exponents −12..12, Weyl sign invariance and kernel-size gcd, rational-power circle surjectivity witnesses, the exact `su(2)` cross-bracket and orthogonality on a finite lattice, a rank-two diagonal `su(3)` control, and central parity on finite `SU(2)` representation sums.

**Total:** **66,977/66,977 PASS**. This is an exact finite control, not a standalone proof of the Peter–Weyl theorem, Lie integration classification, GP2 origin, unique GP3 least structured class or empirical physical B-test occurrence. E1/E3 arguments above provide the general mathematical statements conditional on their actual source hypotheses. Negative controls outside the generated physical grammar are flagged as mathematical boundary attacks, **not countermodels to a constituted primitive role**.

## 48.3 Independent mathematical proof replay (E1 + E3)

### 48.3.1 Scaled quaternion group and the source Hopf circle

For any radius `R>0`, regard a point of the round quaternion sphere as `q=Ru` with `|u|=1`. The operation `q⊙r=R^{-1}qr` is transported multiplication: `Ru⊙Rv=Ruv`, so its identity is `R`, and `q⊙\bar q=R`. It yields a genuine `SU(2)` regular parent without claiming that a generic pre-GP3 candidate state manifold equals this parent.

Paper I-A fixes the common-phase Hopf flow `X_H(q)=iq` and `τ_H^+=2π X_H` with period `2π`; on the identity-through Hopf fiber the subgroup is `e^{it}`. In the **same frozen quaternion model** it is a smooth embedded circle of `SU(2)` with no newly chosen torus axis. The inclusion `ι_H` is injective; for `u,v` in that circle, `uv=vu`, whence `ι_H^op(uv)=uv=vu=ι_H^op(u)⋆_opι_H^op(v)`. On the abelian subalgebra, the opposite-group bracket restriction is the same zero bracket, and `exp_op(tX_H)=exp(tX_H)`. Thus the identical point-map inclusion into the opposite group keeps the primitive parameter.

**Critical type distinction (adversarial E4):** Paper I-A's *opposite marked Hopf copy* uses the **different** map `q↦q^{-1}` and transports `X_H(q)=iq` to `X_H^opp(q)=-qi` in its marked model. It changes the parameter `e^{it}↦e^{-it}`. The II-A identity-on-selected-circle `ι_H^op` is **not** that inversion map. Comparing them without recording their map and source/target sorts would falsely manufacture a handedness contradiction. Paper III-A `p3a:def:clocked-hopf-propagation-doctrine` explicitly uses `ι_H^op` and the contragredient `R_{g^{-1}}` convention so the inherited primitive has the intended sign.

### 48.3.2 Why the scalar sign is conditional but unique *given provenance*

A continuous circle homomorphism is conjugate (after choosing circle coordinates for a **proof**) to `z↦z^n` for integer `n`; continuity and a group law force the integer winding. Its kernel is the roots of unity of order `|n|` if `n≠0`; an injective homomorphism has `n=+1` or `−1`, hence is bijective and a Lie-group isomorphism. On the lattice it acts by the unit `±1`, sending a primitive to a primitive. In II-A `κ_t` is **already supplied as an occurring frozen morphism**, so `e_t=(κ_t)_*τ_H^+` has an unambiguous image and sign relative to that morphism. Replacing `κ_t` by `I∘κ_t` is an alteration of the frozen data, not a symmetry of the same arrow-labelled presentation.

Without `HS_t`, the target scalar group remains unmarked: the two choices `e_t,−e_t` and their duals `e_E,−e_E` give identical integral pairing and `dE∧dt`. This is the explicit Paper II-F inversion countermodel. Neither bare `U(1)`, bare `SU(2)`, the no-character lemma, nor the cocharacter classification supplies the physical Hopf→succession arrow. Nothing here derives seconds, energy calibration, actual evolution, or a physical Hamiltonian. The direct III-A `p3:thm:transported-scalar-orientation` imports exactly this `κ_t` plus the positive Reeb lattice, with calibration kept separate.

### 48.3.3 Stagewise scalar weights and the generated-readout question

For a continuous finite-dimensional **unitary** `U(1)` action, orthogonal eigenspace decomposition gives `ρ(z)=Σ_{n∈F}z^nP_n`. Differentiating in the dimensionless angle gives the self-adjoint integer-weight generator `Q=Σ nP_n`; evaluating its own minimal-root product on each eigenspace proves `∏_{n∈F}(Q−nI)=0`. The kernel of the whole action is `{z:z^n=1 for all n∈F}`, equivalently `{z:z^g=1}` for `g=gcd{|n|:n≠0}`. If the action is faithful then `g=1`, and in particular there must be some nonzero weight; it need **not** have weight `±1`: support `{2,3}` is faithful. `N_s=max|n|` is finite **for that realization only** and cannot be frozen by GP1 across inequivalent possible representations.

For a frozen comparison, let `D` be the actual difference of the compared objects and `ℓ_1,…,ℓ_k` precisely the **licensed consumed tests**. Claim-relative agreement means every `ℓ_j(D)=0` by subtraction and requires no other invisible operator equality. Full zero `D=0` follows if and only if `X↦(ℓ_j(X))` has trivial kernel on the actually occurring difference space. II-C proves the equivalence using a positive frame operator `R^*R`; this is a proof of the *test-separation criterion*, not a licensing of a new apparatus. With diagonal two-outcome effects on a two-level carrier, an offdiagonal traceless Hermitian difference has all measured contractions zero while remaining nonzero. It refutes the stronger **unguarded** inference, not II-A's conditional corollary. II-B supplies source word-defect formulas; II-D separates multiplication and reconstruction errors for actual licensed comparison maps. Neither imports an unperformed measurement.

### 48.3.4 Atlas types and chronological status

`GEN` records a role whose complete output was generated/co-realized, `DL` records discharge only at a later stage, `ROOT` records an actual G0 physical assertion with no earlier proof, and `OBS` records empty/incompatible branches; these tags do not constitute new objects. In particular, a classification of abstract groups in `Abs(Γ)` is not an embedding in `GrpSupp(Γ)` and neither is a physical support `GeoSupp(Γ)`. An exact `Cmp_m` presentation cannot by equality of cardinalities become a microscopic position carrier. Later physical discharge may update *later claim status* while the earlier branch proof remains conditional. This maintains the recursive-GP/FRC branch repaired in Batch 43.

## 49.3 Independent proof replay of the imported exceptional group classification

Paper I-B `p1b:thm:v233-subgroup-sampler-classification` (its current source lines 4820–4889) directly proves the finite-subgroup classification with **nonnegative normalized weights**, not a secretly assumed uniform instrument. Left-averaging a mathematically exact weighted rule over its own subgroup produces a uniform exact rule *for existence*, but does not erase actual frozen physical weights. A cyclic group lies in a circle-plane and misses a linear test. A binary dihedral group has an intrinsic unoriented axis `u`; its degree-two conjugation-axis harmonic `h_u` has squared value one on all of its nodes, but Haar second moment `1/3`, disproving complete `P2` pairing even with nonuniform nonnegative weights. Hence only `2T`, `2O`, `2I` survive. Subgroup inclusions `2T⊂2O,2I` make `2T` least only when frozen role comparison allows **restriction of all retained data**. Paper I-B `p1b:prop:v233-sampling-restriction` (5225–5248) proves image-unitary transport; it explicitly warns that full-node tests and whole left-regular actions may fail to transport. The source II-A roles exclude those data or require their retention in the comparison. `p1b:prop:v233-hopf72` provides the exact non-group 72-node counterexample, showing why design exactness or antipodality alone never creates the finite subgroup.

**Which observable is consumed:** `P2` Hermitian Gram and Dirichlet pairing require polynomial degree four; all `P2` cubic product moments or all `P2 M_f P2` entries require degree six; all real quartic scalar actions on `P2` require degree eight by polarization. The exact design strengths of the exceptional groups are 5, 7, 11. Thus the source's three candidate rows follow. The displayed 2T degree-six discrepancy is `1/12−1/16=1/48`. For `u=q_0²−q_1²`, the exact quartic moments are `τ_{2T}(u⁴)=1/6`, `τ_{2O}(u⁴)=5/48` and `τ_{2I}(u⁴)=σ(u⁴)=3/40`. `2O` and `2I` have respective order 48 and 120, neither divides the other; a cardinality comparator would be a **different** licensed order from subgroup inclusion. Paper II-F `p2c:thm:v268-endpoint-quadratic-iff-design4` (6508–6549) supplies the actual endpoint-POVM degree-four converse, including its crucial nonzero vector-block coefficient `a−(4−a)/3>0`; the theorem has not been replaced by the claim that every arbitrary POVM does so.

## 49.4 Independent E3: algebra, representation and weights

**Recentred 24-cell:** With `Z=aHb^{-1}` and `z=ahb^{-1}`, `z^{-1}Z=bh^{-1}Hb^{-1}=bHb^{-1}`. Since `h^{-1}H=H`, every choice of `z` gives the same embedded subgroup for a fixed presentation and hence the same conjugacy orbit under different lifts. The derivation uses an existing multiplication owner and does not pick an embedded representative physically.

**Full averaging:** `A_μ²=A_μ` on all `C(G)` implies `μ*μ=μ` because evaluation at `e` against all inverted continuous functions separates measures. For finite strictly positive weights this forces `SS=S`, since `supp(μ*μ)=SS`. Every finite nonempty subsemigroup of a group is a subgroup: powers repeat, producing identity and inverse. On this finite subgroup let `a=max_s w_s=w_{s0}`. Idempotence gives `a=∑_g w_g w_{g^{-1}s0}≤a∑_g w_g=a`; all `w_g>0` forces all weights equal to `a=1/|S|`. Conversely normalized subgroup Haar is idempotent.

**Finite-band countermodel:** On `P5`, `μ_{g2T}` and Haar have the same convolution operator because `Sym^k(C²)` has no `2T` invariants for `1≤k≤5`; the restriction is the identity on constants and zero on every nonconstant harmonic. For `g∉2T`, the support `g2T` does not contain the identity and hence cannot be a subgroup. This is not a countermodel to *full-domain* averaging repeatability.

**First leaked degree:** For `k_Γ=6,8,12`, the character sums `|Γ|^{-1}∑_{γ∈Γ}U_k(Re γ)` certify a single `Γ`-fixed line `Cv` in `Sym^{k_Γ}(C²)` and no positive lower-degree invariant. With `P=|v⟩⟨v|` and `T=ρ_k(g)P`, multiplication gives `T²−T=(⟨v,ρ_k(g)v⟩−1)T`. Its vanishing is equivalent, by unitarity, to `ρ_k(g)v=v`; the stabilizer is exactly `Γ` by the compact subgroup classification and exclusion of higher finite groups possessing an invariant in that same degree. This is a *specified uniform-coset* test, not an inference that an unregistered μ is a coset.

**Two support-polynomial moment conditions:** Each `d_s(q)=1−⟨q,s⟩≥0` vanishes exactly at `s` on `S³`. Hence `p_S=∏_s d_s` has zero set `S`, and strictly positive convolution weights make `∫p_S d(μ*μ)=0` iff `SS⊆S`. Under subgroup closure, the nonnegative Lagrange polynomials `ℓ_s` have `ℓ_s(t)=δ_{st}`. Therefore `h_μ=∑_s w_s ℓ_s` interpolates the weights; `∫h_μ dμ=||w||²`, `∫h_μ d(μ*μ)=⟨w,w*w⟩`. The convolution norm contraction plus equality of these two inner products forces `w*w=w`, hence uniformity. For `u_s=1/N`, `x=w−u`, `α=Nw_min`, `w=αu+(1−α)v`, and `w*x=(1−α)v*x`, the defect obeys `δ_h≥α||x||²`, exactly as printed. Likewise leakage `∫p_S d(μ*μ)` bounds products leaving `S` by the printed finite `c_S>0` when they exist. **All these integral and stability statements are valid.**

**Unmarked sixth moment:** `A_6^{supp}(Γ)=||M_6(Γ)−M_6^{Haar}||²`, not a weighted-instrument readout. For a subgroup, pair averaging reduces it to `|Γ|^{-1}∑(Re γ)^6−5/64`: `2T` has `3/32−5/64=1/64`, while exact 7- and 11-designs `2O`/`2I` yield 0. The theorem does not license an experimental support-moment instrument, and an arbitrary positive physical measure need not share the uniform-support moment.

**Loop:** `coev(1)=∑_i e^i⊗e_i` in `H*⊗H`; `ev((1⊗T)coev)=∑_i e^i(Te_i)=Tr T`. For `T=⊕_λt_λI_{H_λ}`, `Tr T=∑d_λt_λ`. Dividing by the identity-loop value `dim H` produces `d_λ/dim H`, with no state preparation. Adding an insertion `B` changes the source word and gives `Tr BT`.

## 50.5 Independent proof of the support-cardinality P_N certificate

For positive `μ=Σ_{s∈S} w_sδ_s`, `|S|=N`, the scalar harmonic band `P_N` contains the inverse pullback of

`p_S(q)=∏_{s∈S}(1−⟨q,s⟩)` (degree N, nonnegative, with exact zero locus S).

Because inversion `q↦q^{-1}` is real orthogonal on quaternionic S³, both `p_S∘inv` and every inverse-pulled interpolation polynomial remain in the same band. The source operator convention `A_μ f(x)=∫ f(g^{-1}x)dμ(g)` then gives

`(A_μ²−A_μ)(p_S∘inv)(e)=∫p_Sd(μ*μ)−∫p_Sdμ = ∑_{s,t}w_sw_t p_S(st)`.

The sum vanishes only if `SS⊆S`, because every weight is positive and every factor is nonnegative. A nonempty finite multiplicatively closed subset of a group is a subgroup. On that group the degree-(N−1) Lagrange tests

`ℓ_s(q)=∏_{t≠s}(1−⟨q,t⟩)/(1−⟨s,t⟩)`

satisfy `ℓ_s(t)=δ_{s,t}`. Inverse-pulled identity-point testing forces `μ*μ=μ` as measures on S. The prior validated positive convolution theorem `p2a:thm:v273-positive-repeatable-convolution-group` (9430–9464) then implies positive uniform Haar weights. Conversely a uniform finite subgroup average is idempotent on all functions. Peter–Weyl decomposes `P_N=⊕_{k=0}^N E_k`, `E_k` carrying the left `Sym^k(C²)` block; thus idempotence on this band is equivalent to `μhat(k)^2=μhat(k)` for each k ≤ N. The sum of `(k+1)||...||²_HS` is basis-independent, nonnegative, and zero exactly when all blocks are idempotent.

**This proof does not consume the false identity-point equivalence of the earlier v274 theorem.** That theorem's *moment* calculations and the earlier full-positive idempotent-measure theorem remain correct, and the v275 text explicitly uses the inverse-pulled functions at 11995–12008. The existing open issue is source-local, not a blocker of this valid independent route.

**E4 exact finite SU(2) torus controls:** for cyclic `C_m`, 2≤m≤8, every positive integer-weight vector on supports of 1≤N≤5 points with coefficients in `{1,2,3}` was exhaustively enumerated — **34,881** cases. With denominator `T=Σa_j` and convolution defect `D(x)=Σ[(a*a)_j−T a_j]x^j`, `Sym^k`-band idempotence is checked *exactly* by reduction modulo `Φ_{m/gcd(m,ℓ)}(x)` for all `ℓ=1,...,N`. Exactly **48** of these instances meet the band criterion, and every one is precisely a uniform subgroup measure; all uniform subgroup measures found in the census pass. This is an adversarial subdomain search, not an exhaustive test of nonabelian SU(2).

### Two-point sharpness

For `g≠±1`, let `S_g={g,-g}` and `μ=(δ_g+δ_{-g})/2`. On `P₁` all nonconstant linear modes cancel (`g` and `−g` act antipodally), so the averaging is the constants projector and is idempotent, despite S omitting the identity. Degree-two `p_{S_g}(q)=1−⟨q,g⟩²` gives exact defect `1−(Re g)²>0`. With `g=i` this is `1` exactly. Thus the printed degree-(N−1) insufficiency witness for N=2 is valid; it does not assert the full N bound is optimally sharp for all N.

## 50.6 Eighth-moment signature and coset-location distinction

Let `σ` be normalized Haar on S³ and `M₈(ν)=∫q^{⊗8}dν(q)`. For a uniform subgroup Γ,

`||M₈(μΓ)−M₈(σ)||²=E_{γ∈Γ}(Re γ)^8−E_{σ}(q_0)^8`, with `E_σ(q_0)^8=7/128`.

This follows by expanding the squared tensor distance, using pair overlaps `⟨g,h⟩=Re(g⁻¹h)` and subgroup averaging. The **exact** real-part multiplicity distributions printed at II-A 12053–12061 then give means `11/128, 15/256, 7/128` for `2T,2O,2I`, respectively, hence the squared-discrepancy values `1/32, 1/256, 0`. The golden-ratio identity `φ⁸+φ⁻⁸=47` independently verifies the `2I` term. This norm is invariant under simultaneous left rotation of the tensor and Haar, so the same values hold for the uniform left coset `gΓ`. They therefore **cannot detect a coset's origin** or prove subgroup support.

With the source's **additional already-known uniform coset hypothesis**, the first leaked symmetric-power degree `k_Γ=6,8,12` (corresponding to the three types) has rank-one averaged invariant-line projector `P=vv*`. The Fourier block is `T=ρ(g)P`, and direct multiplication gives `T²−T=(⟨v,ρ(g)v⟩−1)T`, with `||T||_HS=1`. By the previously verified exact invariant-vector stabilizer result `p2a:thm:v274-first-leaked-harmonic-coset` (9509–9555), its square defect vanishes iff `g∈Γ`. The two diagnostics thus separate **exceptional type** from **coset origin**, in that order. They do not prove the coset hypothesis, physical occurrence, or the presence of a corresponding instrument. Paper I-B `p1b:thm:v274-branch-symmetric-twin-convolution` (5072–5149) supplies the source's non-subgroup degree-11-exact measure `ξ_{H,n}`; its eighth-order tensor matches Haar even while its support is not a subgroup.

**E3/E4:** symbolic rational/Galois exact moment check, `φ`-identity, and exact orthogonal rank-one tensor controls. No new mathematical defect is certified.

## 51.2 E3 proof: clock-normalized characteristic and action relation

Definition `p2a:def:v2190-clocked-phase-word` gives a real one-form `ϑ_t` on the already generated phase role, a real `H_t`, an oriented `t`, actual allowed variations, and **nondegenerate** `Ω_t = −d_Pϑ_t` on the retained regular chart. With `α=ϑ_t−H_t dt` one obtains, by the exterior differential split,

\[
\beta=-d\alpha=\Omega_t+\eta_t\wedge dt,
\qquad \eta_t=\partial_t\vartheta_t+d_{\mathcal P}\mathcal H_t.
\]

For `X=∂_t+Y_t`, contraction yields

\[
\iota_X\beta=(\iota_Y\Omega_t-\eta_t)+\eta_t(Y)dt.
\]

Nondegeneracy gives exactly one `Y_t` solving `ι_YΩ_t=η_t`. Antisymmetry then gives `η_t(Y)=Ω_t(Y,Y)=0`; this proves the **whole** contraction equation and unique clock normalization. Conversely a clock-normalized null vector gives the same spatial equation. The first variation is `δS=∫dα(V,\dotσ)+[α(V)]_{endpoints}`; under the source's **full vertical test** and endpoint term conditions, stationarity is equivalent to `ι_{\dotσ}β=0`, hence to `\dotσ=X`. On restricted variation domains only the projected/tested Euler relation follows, exactly as the source warns.

Because `d_PΩ_t=0` and `∂_tΩ_t=−d_P∂_tϑ_t`, Cartan and `ι_YΩ_t=∂_tϑ_t+d_PH_t` imply

\[
\partial_t\Omega_t+\mathcal L_{Y_t}\Omega_t=0,
\qquad F_{t,s}^{*}\Omega_t=\Omega_s
\]

on the common existence domain. The **time-dependent** energy transport law is

\[
\tfrac{d}{dt}H_t(\sigma(t))=\partial_tH_t-(\partial_t\vartheta_t)(Y_t),
\]

not the generally incorrect autonomous `dH/dt=0`. For `ϑ=p_i dq^i` independent of `t`, this reduces to the displayed Hamilton equations. On C² local coefficients with invertible Ω the field is C¹ and hence locally Lipschitz; standard local Cauchy uniqueness and compact-chart continuation follow. These are **conditional mathematical representations**, not constitutional selection of a physical law or state.

**E4 attack:** symbolic and exact rational families use `ϑ_t=(a_0+a_1t)p dq` and `H_t=p²/(2m)+kq²/2+νt qp+λtq`, so `Y_q=H_p/a`, `Y_p=−(a_1p+H_q)/a`. Exact character, energy and transport identities hold even at nonzero `a_1`. The excluded degenerate four-dimensional `Ω` explicitly has rank two and multiple characteristic directions; it violates the theorem's nondegeneracy premise and therefore is not an in-domain refutation.

## 51.3 Complete-owner isotropy: proved sufficiency, not symmetry by coordinates

For the actual isotropy group `G_m`, theorem `p2a:thm:v2190-clock-isotropy-rigidity` requires both `β,dt` preservation **and** equivariance of the separately licensed `X_op`. The complete pre-generator signature includes the action's source, boundary, field and other retained marks; it is the same-owner rule of `p2a:thm:v368-same-owner-unmarked-equivariance` (2354–2405). Unique clock-normalized characteristic `X` is fixed by every such isotropy automorphism. Therefore `X_op−X∈V_m^{G_m}`. If the fixed space vanishes, equality follows. The affine set `X+V_m^{G_m}` classifies invariant **tangent directions**, not independently generated operations.

In a bare presymplectic chart with transverse symplectic matrix `Ω`, let `η=−ΩY` and

\[
\beta=\begin{pmatrix}\Omega&\eta\\-\eta^{T}&0\end{pmatrix},
\quad X=\begin{pmatrix}Y\\1\end{pmatrix},
\quad g=\begin{pmatrix}-I&2Y\\0&1\end{pmatrix}.
\]

Then `βX=0`, `g^Tβg=β`, `dt\,g=dt`, and `gX=X` **exactly**. This shows the sufficient `−I` transverse symmetry for the **bare comparison tensors**. The source itself and Paper II-F `p2a:app:v2190-exactness-stationarity` (108–156) and `p2a:prop:v2190-retained-mark-fixed-vector` (158–174) immediately prevent importing that bare transformation into a marked owner without checking its marks. If every actual isotropy fixes a nonzero `ℓ∈V*`, its Ω-dual `Z` is a nonzero fixed vector, so `V^G≠0` and the zero-fixed-space premise fails. In particular the differential of a retained nonconstant Hamiltonian can obstruct bare inversion. A shear `[[1,s],[0,1]]` preserves both `dq∧dp` and `dp`, and fixes `∂_q`; this is a controlled boundary example.

**Verdict:** No overclaim of automatic same-owner isotropy was found. The Paper II-F diagnostic explicitly states that symmetry invariance of `S` along symmetry directions is weaker than stationarity along all allowed variations. `X_op=∂_t` and `H=p`, `ϑ=p dq` yield a nonzero residual and demonstrate why operation provenance alone cannot replace the identification certificate.

This is a finite **certificate**, not a new test or law unless the corresponding readout is licensed. ## 52.2 Exact A1 globalization, eigenvalues, spectral observables and chronological order

At the same Lie algebra `su(2)`, the universal connected integration is `SU(2)` with centre `{±I}`. Connected Hausdorff Lie globalizations correspond to discrete central kernels, so exactly the trivial and order-two kernel occur: `SU(2)` and `SO(3)=SU(2)/{±I}`. This is a mathematical comparison of global forms, **not** the candidatewise GP2 endpoint proof from earlier B42/B47.

For a round radius `R`, scalar harmonics of degree `k` on `S³` have eigenvalues `k(k+2)/R²`, multiplicity `(k+1)²`, and antipodal parity `(-1)^k`. The quotient `RP³` allows only even `k`. For `k=1` on `SU(2)` the first nonconstant eigenspace has `(λ,dim)=(3/R²,4)`. For `k=2` on `SO(3)` it has `(λ,dim)=(8/R²,9)`. Both local scalar curvatures equal `6/R²`, so scale-free ratios are `1/2` and `4/3`. Exact rational checks at radii `1/7,1/2,1,3/2,12` confirm the scale cancellation, and 49 harmonic degrees confirm the algebraic spectrum/parity formulas.

The first scalar harmonic thus provides a **testable discriminator** *conditional on an actual earlier licensed scalar Laplace readout in the role*. Neither `G0` by itself nor GP2's non-insertion rule licenses that test after inspecting an `SU(2)` atlas. A nontrivial compact connected circle satisfies the bare G0-level existence grammar and is abelian, so G0 alone cannot force a noncommuting role. This is not an attack on the earlier constitutive primitive role and candidatewise endpoint proof; it identifies the correct quantifier and temporal order.

## 52.3 A5 projective rank-two calculation independently reproduced

The direct controlling imported source is **Paper I-A** `Paper1A-Geometry-v5_162.tex`, theorems `p1:thm:v340-cyclic-coset-design`, `p1:thm:v340-incidence-character-projectors`, `p1:thm:v340-reye-70-80`, `p1:thm:v341-coset-gram-hodge`, and `p1:thm:v341-desargues-rp2-parity`. **Paper II-F** `Paper2F-TechnicalMaterial-v1_57.tex`, `p2f:prop:v340-projective-binary-orthogonality` and `p2f:rem:v340-ranktwo-proof-scope`, supplies the projective/binary bridge and explicit physical caution. These are inspected **as direct imports**, not globally reaudited peer papers.

The verifier independently constructs all 60 even permutations of five letters as `A5=2I/{±1}`, all element orders, all cyclic subgroups of orders 2,3,5 and all their left cosets. It gives:

| Cyclic order | Number of subgroups | Projective cosets | Binary great polygons |
|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 2 | 15 | 450 | squares |
| 3 | 10 | 200 | hexagons |
| 5 | 6 | 72 | decagons |
| **Total** | **31** | **722** | |

Every unordered pair of the 60 projective points belongs to exactly one of these cosets; integer incidence matrices satisfy `M₂+M₃+M₅=30I₆₀+J₆₀` in **all 3,600 positions**, and the pair count is `450+200·3+72·10=1770=C(60,2)`. Exact integer squared identities hold for `N₅=M₂−M₃` and `N₄=M₃−M₅`:

`N₅²=12N₅`, `N₄²=15N₄`, `N₅N₄=0`, `Tr(N₅)=300`, `Tr(N₄)=240`.

Therefore `P₅=N₅/12` and `P₄=N₄/15` are orthogonal projectors of **rank 25** and **rank 16**. The rank-25 image is *not* the 25-element transporter-label span: the 60×25 projective transporter matrix `B_tr` has rank 17 and `N₅ B_tr=0`. In the original 120-point binary lift the normalized centre-even pullback doubles the unnormalized Gram by a factor of two; no mixing of projective/binary counting conventions is justified.

For the five point-stabilizer `A4` cores, their union is the 36-element set of identity, involutions and 3-cycles. Exactly 70 order-three cosets lie in at least one core, while 80 have all three vertices in the union. The difference is the ten `(2,2,2)` mixed-core hexagons. The 70-plane incidence matrix has exact integral left nullvector `z=-2` on order-1/2 points and `z=1` on order-3 points. Independent elimination modulo 1009 gives rank at least 35; the nullvector gives rational rank at most 35. Hence `rank_Q R70=35`. The 80-plane incidence matrix has mod-1009 rank 36, hence `rank_Q R80=36` (all rows), exactly confirming the source's missing-ten-plane rank increase.

## 52.4 Signed Hodge and Desargues parity: mathematical marks versus physical effects

On the ten unordered pairs of five labels, the disjoint-pair adjacency `A` is Petersen. Build the signed Hodge `S_H` using orientation of the unique complementary fifth index for each disjoint pair pair. Integer checks prove `S_H³=5 S_H`. The source's second-neighbour operator `T=S_H²−3I` satisfies `T(S_H)=T(−S_H)` identically, so it loses the sign needed to separate the two signed-parity channels. This is an exact nonidentifiability observation, not an instruction to add a new parity postulate.

The Desargues bipartite cover has block adjacency `[[0,A],[A,0]]` and spectrum `3^(1),2^(4),1^(5),(-1)^(5),(-2)^(4),(-3)^(1)`. For **all 120 permutations** of five labels, the induced ordinary permutation action preserves `A`; the oriented exterior-square action sends `S_H` to `sgn(π)S_H`. In particular an odd permutation changes the signing while exchanging two `RP²` face sets. **This is transport between marked mathematical presentations**, not a symmetry of one frozen signed physical owner without the requisite action/effect certificate.

A proposed physical outcome would need (i) an occurring geometric packet, (ii) an independently licensed same-owner preparation/effect and its embedding, (iii) normalized measurable probabilities/poles, and (iv) a quantitative difference between actual allowed competitor supports/signings. None follows from the rank numerology or graph spectrum. The rank-15 finite coset projector also remains distinct from the intrinsic complex `H₁` dimension 15; none of this changes `m_p=1`.

### Independent mathematical rederivation

Define the injective evaluation representation `j(e)(x)=ε(x,e)`. A putative dual `T^∨` must satisfy `j(T^∨e)=P_T(j(e))`, where `P_T f=f∘T`. Thus `P_T(j(E))⊆j(E)` is **necessary and sufficient**. Under this condition, `j^{-1}` is well-defined only on `j(E)` and gives the unique dual. Since `P_{T∘U}=P_U P_T`, the family of stabilizing maps is a monoid and dualization reverses composition. This mathematical existence proof does **not** supply the frozen physical precomposition license, typed target or co-realized root. Those enter independently through the stated origin certificate and the rooted GP2 theorem. The distinction prevents the conventional mathematical dual from being silently declared a new physical process.

For host coverage, induct on the finite length of the word. The result is an implication **under host compatibility of every required arrow**, not an unconditional theorem for arbitrary set-theoretic functions. Two distinct functions on `{0,1}`, `f(x)=x` and `g(x)=1−x`, may both have outputs hosted in one `H={0,1}`; common hosting does not entail same output. For nonseparating evaluation, `X={*}`, `E={a,b}` and `ε(*,a)=ε(*,b)=0`, even the identity on `X` admits four possible maps `E→E` preserving all evaluations; evaluation separation is indispensable for uniqueness. With `X={0,1}`, test family `E={x↦x}` and `T(x)=1−x`, `f∘T∉E`, so no dual can be generated without an enlarged test family/typed constructor.

## 53.2 GP3 principle and repeated-label reconciliation

The `p2:post:GP3` source ~3732–3814 is **foundational postulate content**, already examined in prior batches, **not a new independently proved theorem**. It defines complete structured GP1/GP2 candidate comparison, distinguishes `least` from `minimal`, and prevents a raw carrier-size/operation-count selector. In a nonempty preorder, a least class, if established, is irreducible; where a distinct survivor family is claimed, its nonemptiness must also be justified. In a general infinite preorder, existence of a minimal/irreducible element does **not** follow from GP1 finiteness of each candidate. Example: candidates indexed by positive integers, with `M_j≺M_i` for `j>i`, have no irreducible member. This is a **scope challenge**, not a new in-domain refutation of the primitive endpoints: their already audited candidatewise Lie-image/exactification route establishes an actual least skeleton under the stated role hypotheses. No new issue is certified without an actual consumer relying on nonexistent minimal members; the retained common-readout theorems already require a nonempty surviving family.

## 53.3 Repair of `BH-IIA-CONV-ID-001` (previously OPEN)

**Actual source mismatch in v3.210, `p2a:thm:v274-two-scalar-repeatability-certificate` lines 9578–9580:** its two displayed valid mathematical moment tests were called *equivalent* to repeatability of the same two polynomial functions under the left averaging action at the identity, even though `A_μ f(x)=∫f(g^{-1}x)dμ(g)`. The correct identity is

`A_μ²(f∘inv)(e) = ∫ f d(μ*μ)`, `A_μ(f∘inv)(e) = ∫ f dμ`.

**Source repair v3.211:** qualify the identity-point tests as `p_S∘inv` and `h_μ∘inv`, give both displayed identities, and explicitly reject the old claim for the unmodified functions. Quaternion inversion is an orthogonal linear map on the ambient R⁴, so the respective degrees `N` and `≤N−1` are unchanged. **Both moment conditions and their quantitative bounds remain unchanged.** No independent function-valued test becomes physically available without a licensed same-owner test/averaging role. The B49 exact C5 counterexample to the old gloss is retained as an adversarial regression: `μ=δ_a`, `a^5=e`, `a≠e`, with polynomial `p_S(q)=1−⟨q,a⟩`, has `A_μ p_S(e)=A_μ²p_S(e)=(5+√5)/4`, although `∫p_S d(μ*μ)=(5−√5)/4>0` and `{a}` is not a group. The revised inverse-pulled test correctly fails. The B50 finite-band theorem's inverse-pulled proof remains an **independent intact bypass**.

**Issue disposition:** `BH-IIA-CONV-ID-001` is now **RESOLVED IN SOURCE (v3.211)**, not a theorem saying every finitely supported convolution operation physically occurs. The old incorrect historical route remains archived as a **blocked v3.210 route**, while a new corrected v3.211 identity route is verified. The other eight prior issue records remain resolved within their existing scopes. **Zero tracked paper issues remain open after this source repair**; potential gaps in unreviewed steps or other papers are not thereby ruled out.

But Paper II-B's `p2b:thm:momentum-carrier-rigidity` (1436–1484) explicitly states that the stronger quantified conclusion `every GP3-irreducible full structured carrier H_p ≅ H_1` requires **three independent, whole-structured comparison certificates**, not just the common six-type support. \[
 \operatorname{Gen}_{\Sigma_p}^{\mathrm{car}}\cong\mathcal H_1
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad m_p=1,
\]

with the separate guarded implication

\[
 [\mathrm{CMP}(i)\land\mathrm{CMP}(ii)\land\mathrm{CMP}(iii)]

 \bigl(\text{every GP3-irreducible full endpoint }\mathcal H_p\cong\mathcal H_1\bigr).
\]

## 3. Independent derivations and adversarial controls

### A. Bracket image and the generated six types

For `S³(R)` and `f_u(x)=u·x`, the exact Riemannian gradient is `G_u=u−(u·x)x/R²`. Differentiating in the ambient coordinates and antisymmetrizing on the sphere yields

\[
 [G_u,G_v](x)=R^{-2}\bigl((u\cdot x)v-(v\cdot x)u\bigr).
\]

Hence the **linear span**, not the literal two-variable image, is canonically `Λ²R⁴ ≅ so(4)` (real dimension 6), which splits after complexification as `(1,0)⊕(0,1)`. Its co-generated first scalar, spinor and gradient modules yield `H_1=1⊕S_+⊕S_-⊕E_1⊕K_{Kill,C}` with dimension 15. Whether those object and operation sorts are physically retained is governed by the frozen II-A/II-B constructor grammar, not this differential identity alone.

### B. Minimal level does not prove universal one-copy realization

If the irreducible types are `T_p`, a general finite representation with *this exact type-support* can have multiplicities `m_λ≥1` and

\[
 \dim\mathcal H-15=\sum_{\lambda\in T_p}d_\lambda(m_\lambda-1),\qquad
 \dim\operatorname{End}_{\mathrm{Spin}(4)}\mathcal H-6
 =\sum_{\lambda\in T_p}(m_\lambda^2-1).
\]

For example, `H_1⊕(0,0)` still has six type classes, `m_p=1`, and full dimension 16 (rather than 15); its equivariant commutant dimension is 9 (rather than 6). The character-squared diagnostic equals `Σ(m_λ−1)²`. These are **mathematical counterexamples to the inference “same support ⇒ one copy.” They are not certified physical candidates**: a GP2 exactness test and the frozen role's complete test/operation diagrams would still have to be checked. Their utility is to demonstrate precisely why Paper II-B has three stronger comparison hypotheses. The framework's observable question is: *Which licensed same-owner tests or constructors can discriminate the multiplicity sectors, and is the universal generated comparison preserving all of them?* Absent such evidence, neither a new physical multiplicity nor universal absence of it is selected.

### C. Inverse/dual and independent joint support

On a concrete unit-quaternion subgroup `Q₈={±1,±i,±j,±k}`, let first-harmonic evaluation represent `a` by its four coordinate values. This separates points. For any `g`, the induced evaluation-dual action is `g∨(a)=g^{-1}a`, and `((gh)∨)(a)=h∨(g∨(a))`. The possible joint pairs are `8×8=64`, whereas a graph of one chosen role isomorphism has only eight points. This is a finite algebraic check of the **independence-versus-graph logical distinction**, not a derivation of physical product occurrence from `Q₈` or of arbitrary marked `2I` support.

### D. Exact reproducibility

`Paper2A-CrossPaperProofBatch54-v1_1-verify.py` reports **23,916/23,916 PASS** deterministic rational/integer assertions: 512 quaternion associativity, 64 closure and inverse cases, 512 dual anti-composition and 512 norm checks, 64 point-separation and 64 total pairings, 810 exact gradient-bracket calculations and 810 tangency checks, and 4,096 multiplicity vectors each checked for type support, dimension excess, equivariant commutant, endpoint one-copy criterion and character defect, plus source/import/label guards. The tests confirm only these **finite mathematical** facts and **source-check contracts**; they do not verify arbitrary infinite GP3 candidate comparisons or the occurrence of a physical detector.
